# Roadmap and known gaps

Status: Western Front, 6 June 1944 to 8 May 1945 for US Army army-group, army, corps and selected division milestones (Batch 4 added 1 January to 31 March 1945; Batch 5 added 1 April to 8 May 1945 on the branch `batch5-apr-may-1945`; the spot check is done and Scott answered the four open points on 27 October 2026, so the branch is ready for his merge); divisions are complete only for the milestones listed in the Batch 3, Batch 4 and Batch 5 sections. German Batch 1 (30 June to 31 August 1944, Scott approved on 27 October 2026) is done on the branch `german-batch1-jul-aug-1944` (52 claims, `C0713` to `C0764`; see the German Batch 1 section). Batch 1 of the US Army fill-in (1 July to 30 September 1944: army group, army, corps and division level) is in, on top of the original 6-30 June proof of concept: 764 claims after German Batch 1 (712 after Batch 5), 359 units (156 active); see `docs/coverage.md`.

For a new maintainer: [HANDOFF.md](HANDOFF.md) (state, locations, standing rules, conventions, checks, decision log, next steps).

## US Army fill-in: progress and plan

Scope (owner-approved): US Army ground units on the Western Front, 6 June 1944 to 8 May 1945, at army group, army, corps and division level; no Marines. Work goes by period with a spot-check gate between batches ([`review/spot_check_batch1.md`](review/spot_check_batch1.md) for Batch 1).

**Not covered in Batch 1 (known gaps):**
- Regiments, combat commands, separate battalions, cavalry groups and corps troops. Batch 1 is division level and up.
- Corps rows are milestones from the Green Books and Wikipedia, so they are sparse. Batch 2 (command transitions, 25 July to 15 September 1944) added rows for V, VII, VIII, XII, XV, XIX and XX Corps and for First Army on 1 August; outside that window the corps gaps remain, and the Seventh Army place on 15 September is still unlocated.
- Army and army group headquarters places are mostly not sourced; those rows use a representative point at 50 km and confidence 0.3 (see the Batch 1 rules in `docs/methodology.md`).
- CP-derived division rows use CMH command-post towns; towns that could not be geocoded safely were dropped, so some dates have no row (see [`review/review_changes.md`](review/review_changes.md)).
- The 6 US units that were still placeholders on 26 October 2026 after Batch 4 and the XXII/XXIII Corps task (13th, 16th and 20th Armored; 71st, 86th and 97th Infantry) were activated by Batch 5 (27 October 2026); no US unit that has claims is a placeholder now (the 217 placeholders left are German, British, Canadian, French, Polish, Belgian, Dutch, Czechoslovak and Allied headquarters units, and two US corps records added on 27 October 2026 at Scott's approval, X Corps and XIV Corps, which exist only to give the CMH table entries an id; the sources read place both corps in the Pacific, so their theater is `pacific`). (Batch 4 made 19 of the 27 active: XVI Corps, Fifteenth Army, 17th Airborne, 8th, 9th, 11th, 12th and 14th Armored, 42nd, 63rd, 65th, 66th, 69th, 70th, 76th, 78th, 89th, 95th and 99th Infantry.) The 26th and 44th Divisions have only their arrival rows here.
- The 82nd and 101st Airborne rows cover England and Market Garden's first days only; the later fighting in Holland is Batch 2.
- German opponents are named only where a Green Book page states them; most US rows have an empty `engaged_with`. (Opponents are unit ids now; a named opponent missing from `data/units.csv` becomes a placeholder.)

## Known gaps in the current data

**Coverage left out (no verified dates or too thin sources):**
- British XII Corps (arrival date not found), II Canadian Corps and First Canadian Army (July); 12th Army Group HQ place (operational 1 August; Batch 1 gives a representative point only).
- Free French, 1st Special Service Brigade and the 2nd Canadian Armoured Brigade after D-Day.
- German Fifteenth Army, Luftwaffe units, the 319th Division (Channel Islands), 85th, 89th and 116th Panzer (arrive July).
- (US 5th, 8th, 28th and 35th Divisions and 4th Armored were covered in Batch 1.)
- Battle groups: Hellmich (formed about 9-10 June), Hoffmann, Keil and Mueller (Cotentin, undated in the source), and the US 4th Division task forces are sourced but not yet added because the date or place was too vague.
- Roughly 40-60 German units that fought in the sector (regiments, Kampfgruppen, Ost battalions) are only partially represented.

**Data quality:**
- Rows are event-based; quiet days are absent, so positions between rows are unknown.
- Many corps, army and panzer-corps headquarters positions are not tied to a dated source (inferred or approximate). HQ moves are poorly covered by free web sources.
- Composition lists are the standard organisation checked against Wikipedia unit pages, not the exact attachments each day. Not verified: US 2nd and 3rd Armored combat commands, 21st Panzer "Panzer Regiment 22", 79th Armoured and independent brigades (marked `unknown`).
- `parent_unit_id` and `command_relationships` are time-dependent and some links are only supported in outline (for example I SS Panzer Corps under Seventh Army or Panzer Group West; II SS Panzer Corps on 28-29 June). Where no link was found both are `unknown` (16 rows). Plain parents are recorded as `organic` because the sources do not say whether a corps or army link was a standing one; attachments are marked only where a source says so.
- `precision_km` and `confidence` are rule-derived (see methodology); they are consistent but not independent measurements.
- `strength_json` is sparse (20 rows) and rests on secondary sources; no row uses `authorized_organization` yet.
- LXXXIV Corps rows C0106, C0116, C0123 and C0128 (14-17 June) used an unverified Cotentin placeholder point (49.52,-1.47, 25 km; PL0102); no source read places the corps headquarters on those dates (CMH only says Rommel visited "LXXXIV Corps headquarters" on 16 June). **Done on 26 October 2026 (branch `open-items-oct26`):** the four rows now use the corps' Saint-Lô point as an annotated inferred point and PL0102 is removed (see `docs/changelog.md`). The same placeholder on C0067 was replaced in the G004 fix (the conflict there was about the handover date, not the place; see [`review/review_changes.md`](review/review_changes.md)).
- Date conflicts between sources are open (Dollmann's death, Schlieben's capture, Choltitz replacing Fahrmbacher, Montgomery's HQ moves, 82nd Airborne at Pont-l'Abbe): see the 11 conflict groups.
- Wikipedia is a first-pass source; many rows should be re-verified against primary or semi-primary sources.

## Placeholder units: what is not in the table yet

The placeholder pass (see "Placeholder units" in `docs/methodology.md`) is a first, source-listed set; it is not a complete order of battle.

**Left out on purpose (to do next):**
- Air and naval units (the vocabularies reserve `air_force`, `air_formation`, `fleet`, `naval_unit` and the branches `Air Force`, `Navy` and `Marines`), then the other theaters and Italy.
- Regiments, battalions, separate US tank, tank destroyer, artillery and engineer groups, British Army Groups Royal Artillery and Armoured Brigades beyond those listed, Special Air Service brigade, First Allied Airborne Army and the Atlantic-pocket commands (Detachment of the Army of the Atlantic), the French Detachment of the Army of the Alps, the 13th Airborne and 98th Infantry Divisions (not found in the listing sources), Polish I Corps (role in the West to be checked).
- German: naval ground units, Volkswerfer, assault gun and Heeres artillery brigades, Sicherungs and fortress units, the Schnelle Brigaden 20 and 30, Panzer-Brigaden 109 and 110, 20th and 21st Fallschirmjaeger Divisions and the 12th Army and Armeegruppe Student (service in the West uncertain or only a name for another record), III Flak Corps, and Divisions in Denmark and Norway.

**Ambiguities recorded in `unit_note` or `component_of_note`:**
- German reorganisations: Reserve Corps renamed Armeekorps on 5 August 1944 (LXII, LXIV, LXVI); Korpsgruppe/Generalkommando Kniess becoming LXXXV Corps; Panzer Group West becoming Fifth Panzer Army (separate records); Infantry divisions destroyed and re-formed as Volksgrenadier divisions (separate records); the 19th Luftwaffe Field Division / Luftwaffen-Sturm-Division and the later 19th Volksgrenadier Division; Fuehrer-Begleit and Fuehrer-Grenadier brigades becoming divisions in January 1945; the Lexikon lists a "4. Armee" in Army Group B in November 1944, which looks like an error and is not used.
- French 1st Division naming (1st Free French Division / 1re Division de Marche d'Infanterie), the 11th Army (Western Front only in April 1945), the 24th Army (a skeleton at the Swiss frontier) and Armee-Abteilung von Luettwitz.
- 1st Airborne Task Force and 1st Special Service Force (provisional, mixed-nationality formations) are not in the table.
- The 6 June 1944 German list shows the 326th Division under two corps, and the 271st, 272nd and 277th after Korpsgruppe Kniess without a corps heading; they have no 6 June parent, and the parent shown is the first matching Lexikon timeline entry (see `component_of_note`).

**Next steps for the unit table:** reconcile Allied formation dates against CMH lineage and the British Joslen volumes (many Allied dates are blank because the Wikipedia infobox starts with a First World War lineage); add the Allied parent chain by dated sources; add regiments and Kampfgruppen as claims need them; extend `listing_source` tokens when new sources are used.

## Scale-up plan

1. **Stable unit and place tables.** Keep `data/units.csv` as the unit authority (add formation and disbandment dates, alternative names, higher parent chain) and add a place table with coordinates, historical names and source, referenced by claims.
2. **Better sources for order of battle**: US Army Green Books (CMH), Joslen's *Orders of Battle* (British), the Canadian official history, German OOB references (Tessin, *Lexikon der Wehrmacht*, axishistory), and Soviet *Boevoi sostav* lists. Use Wikipedia as a first pass only.
3. **Per-row evidence**: add a quote or page reference (not only a URL) and a separate `coordinate_source` column, plus a coordinate precision type (point / town / sector), so a coordinate can be re-checked.
4. **Batch geocoding with a cache** (GeoNames or Wikidata with Nominatim as a fallback; `data/places.csv` has no `wikidata_id` or `geonames_id` column yet because none is known, add them when the ids are looked up) and a gazetteer with historical names (places change names, for example Octeville / Cherbourg-Octeville).
5. **New theaters and campaigns**: new theaters get their own `theater` value and folders stay by nationality, so contributors can work independently. Crawl each campaign page and its OOB page, then run a verification pass that checks date, place, unit and echelon against two sources before a row is accepted.
6. **Weaker source bases**: the Eastern Front and the Pacific need Russian and Japanese-language sources and atlases (Glantz-type), so expect lower confidence and more conflict groups.
7. **Tooling**: a dataset browser (map and timeline) generated from `build/claims.geojson`, CSV and GeoJSON releases with a changelog, a Frictionless data package descriptor, and unit tests for the scripts.
8. **Community**: issue and PR templates, a review rota for source checking, and tagged releases with a changelog.

## Proposed changes after the first spot check (not implemented: they change the schema or the process)

The review guardrails in `docs/methodology.md` (rules, `scope`, `effective_from`, validator warnings, `scripts/check_coordinates.py`, the per-row checklist) are in place and are additive. These larger changes were considered and left as proposals; each has a recommendation.

| proposal | why | change | recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Make `strength_json.scope` mandatory when `personnel` is present | whole-unit versus sub-force figures were confused (C0057); 12 existing rows lacked a scope | turn the validator warning into an error after backfilling those rows | **Done.** All rows now have a scope; `scripts/validate.py` reports an error for `personnel` without `scope` (schema README, methodology and CONTRIBUTING updated). Last two rows resolved: C0027 (4th Infantry Division) is now 20,000 with scope `sector`, because CMH counts everything landed at Utah Beach, not the division; for C0018 (6th Airborne Division) sources give 7,000, 8,500 and about 12,000 without saying what each covers, so the figure was removed (see [`review/review_changes.md`](review/review_changes.md)) |
| A `command_relations` table (unit, related unit, relationship, start, end, source) instead of the JSON cell | attachments are time-dependent and JSON in CSV is hard to query or review; `effective_from` is only a stop-gap | new table, claims keep a derived `parent_unit_id`; breaking | **Done** on branch `command-relations-table` (October 2026): `data/command_relations.csv` is the master (371 relations), `data/command_relation_claims.csv` the evidence (1,076 links); `parent_unit_id` stays on the claim and is validated against the relations (41 of 566 values cannot be derived by a rule). See `docs/changelog.md`. |
| Optional `verification_status`, `reviewed_by`, `reviewed_on` columns on claims | to know which rows were independently checked and to sample the rest by risk | additive nullable columns | **Yes**, soon: cheap and it turns spot checks into coverage |
| Per-row `evidence` (quote or page reference) and `coordinate_source` columns | a reviewer re-checks a row faster, and a coordinate can be traced (already in the scale-up plan) | additive nullable columns | **Yes**, high value |
| Place table with a geometry type (point, line, area) and a representative point | a front line or sector (the Elle line) is not a point; midpoint plus radius is a workaround | new `places` table referenced by claims; breaking | **Done** (27 October 2026, branch `places-table`): `data/places.csv` with kind point / area / line, 383 places, `place_id` on every claim; see [places_review.md](places_review.md) for the open merge decisions |
| Action-level rows (a separate `actions` table or structured `action_type` and `counterparty` fields) instead of free-text `current_activity` and `engaged_with` | free text let two actions blur into one (C0184) | new fields or table; breaking | **No for now**: one-row-one-action plus the warnings catches most cases; revisit if aggregation recurs |
| Make the coordinate bounding-box check an error and run the offline coordinate script in CI | catches typos and sign errors automatically | CI change, no data change | **Yes** for the bounding box (error); keep the other coordinate checks as warnings |
| Second-source rule for risky rows (attached relationships, strength figures, conflict groups) | these rows carried most of the review findings | process rule, no schema change | **Yes**: already described in the spot-check section; make it a release gate |
| Review the migrated `organic` corps links of divisions and brigades that were attached (found on the 82nd Airborne, C0131) | the migration copied the old `parent_formation` as `organic`, but the sources describe these as operational attachments | **Done** for the 82nd and 101st Airborne (VII Corps, VIII Corps), the 4th Armoured Brigade, the 31st Tank Brigade and the 79th Division (attached to VII Corps from 29 May, under VIII Corps from 28 June; standing parent Third Army, added as a `parent_only` unit): now `attached` or `operational_control`, with `effective_from` where a source gives a date (see [`review/review_changes.md`](review/review_changes.md)). Checked and left `organic` because the sources describe an assignment or a passing of control, not an attachment: the 90th Division (VII Corps from 27 March, VIII Corps from 19 June), the 29th Division (XIX Corps from 14 June; `effective_from` added), the 9th Division and the 1st and 2nd Infantry Divisions | **Remaining, next pass**: the 30th Division (CMH says XIX Corps became operational on 13 June "with the 30th Division attached", Wikipedia lists it as assigned to XIX Corps from 18 February; sources conflict, left `organic`); the 83rd Division (C0134, 18 June, parent `unknown`; sources give attached to VIII Corps only from 25 June); the 2nd and 3rd Armored Divisions and the 4th Infantry Division (not checked against an assignment source); the 6th Airborne Division (standing parent I Airborne Corps, operational parent I Corps); the 8th Armoured Brigade (sources say only "supported"). Change only where a source states the attachment |

## Review of the unit ids in the text columns (26 October 2026)

Items 1 to 7 of `docs/unit_references_review.md` are done on branch `review-items-1-7` (see `docs/changelog.md`): the six `attached:` entries and the 17 other uses of the word are relations or rows of `docs/deferred_relations.md`, the word is prohibited in `composing_text` and `engaged_note`, the ten embedded ids are qualified entries, `First Army` (C0039) stays text, CR0061 is `attached`, `battalion` is an echelon and the id letter convention is documented and checked. Open: item 8 (the five place merges, `docs/places_review.md`); the relationships of `docs/deferred_relations.md` (11 rows) wait until the regiments, battalions and the squadron become units of the table (below division scope); a source that names the army of C0039.

## Batch 1 review proposals (25 October 2026)

A second reviewer checked the 15-row Batch 1 spot check. Their schema and process proposals, with what was done (details in [`review/review_changes.md`](review/review_changes.md), "Systemic changes"):

| proposal | outcome |
|---|---|
| Relationship vocabulary: add `assigned`, `administrative_control`, `supporting`, `coordinating`, `unspecified_subordination`; narrow `organic`; optional `effective_to` and `date_basis` | **Done** (additive). Validator warns when `organic` is used for a division, corps or army under a higher headquarters. All 482 claims were passed through the rule: table-derived links became `unspecified_subordination`, other standing links `assigned`. |
| Systematic pass of army-to-army-group, corps-to-army, division-to-corps and division-to-army links | **Done by rule**, not row by row: sources were only re-read for the 15 spot-checked rows and the rows named in the review. Links that a narrative source states as a standing assignment are `assigned`; the CMH tables are `unspecified_subordination`. Whether individual older division-to-corps links are really attachments remains the to-do already listed above. |
| `location_date`, `location_basis` columns (the offset column was derived from them on 27 October 2026) | **Done** (optional, additive; schema, README, build, validator, all claim files and the GeoJSON). A separate `location_confidence` column was **not** added: `confidence`, `precision_km` and the basis already carry it, and a fifth location field would be bloat. |
| Backfill of CP-derived rows | **Done** for the Batch 1 rows (49 rows have a coordinate from a different date; 128 are same-day CP lists). **Not done** for older rows: their notes do not say where the coordinate came from, so they stay blank. |
| Validator checks (date mismatch, landing with unrelated point, centroid HQ at corps+, front-line point at corps/army, organic misuse, HQ without id, different event definitions in a conflict group) | **Done**, as warnings except the two errors (offset mismatch, landing from a town centroid or CP of another date). The reviewer's check "confidence shared by rows with different evidence" was **not** implemented: it has no clear, testable definition. |
| `event_definition` | **Done as a notes convention** (`Event definition:`), no new column. |
| Notes convention for separate sources | **Done** (`Event date:`, `Relationship:`, `Location:`, `Coordinates:`); applied to the 15 reviewed rows only. |
| Scripted audit of the 268 new rows | **Done**: `scripts/audit_command_location.py`. Its geocoding check found a wrong place (Mesnil-St-Martin) that three rows used. |
| Batch 2 (V, VII, XII, XV, XX Corps command transitions, 25 July to 15 September 1944; Brittany army transitions) | **Done** (authorised 25 October 2026); see the Batch 2 section below and [`review/review_changes.md`](review/review_changes.md). |

## Batch 2 review proposals (25 October 2026)

A third reviewer checked the 15-row Batch 2 spot check. Their findings were verified against the Green Books, the Third Army After Action Report and other sources before anything was applied ([`review/review_changes.md`](review/review_changes.md), "Batch 2 review corrections"). The design proposals and what was done:

| proposal | outcome |
|---|---|
| (a) Explicit validity intervals; a missing `effective_to` must not mean indefinitely valid; optional `ordered_date` | **Done** (additive, inside the relationship JSON): keys `ordered_date`, `effective_from_latest`, `effective_to_earliest`, `effective_to_basis`, validated; the validator prints a count of `attached`/`operational_control` entries with no end that the unit's next claim no longer lists, and the audit lists them (33 claims); explicit ends added where a source dates them (XV Corps, 2nd Armored, 6th Armored, VIII Corps' First Army attachment). The 33 open entries stay as snapshots: the derived table closes them at the next claim. Applied to C0425, C0426 and C0296 (order versus effect). |
| (b) `command_relations` table | **Done**: the master table `data/command_relations.csv` (since the `command-relations-table` branch; before, `build/command_relations.csv` was derived from the JSON cell for US units from 24 July to 31 December 1944). `build/command_relations.csv` now covers every nationality and date with a claim (356 rows, preferred relations except `organic`, 21 `unknown` placeholders) and is queried with `scripts/who_controls.py`. Claims stay the evidence (`data/command_relation_claims.csv`). **Not done**: a numeric relationship confidence (the `support` column says documented, table or inferred instead), dating the five undated German parent changes (flagged `change_undated`), a sourced end for the 53 relations with `end_unknown`. |
| (c) Separate event confidence and location confidence / `location_status` | **Declined again**, with the reasoning in `schema/README.md`: `confidence` already scores the location and its sources; a status column would repeat `location_basis`; a second numeric column would need a judged value for each of the 515 rows. Instead command rows from Batch 2 on carry `Event support:` and `Location support:` sentences (qualitative), the validator warns when a low-confidence command row lacks `Event support:`, and the derived table carries relationship support. Revisit if the derived table needs an event score as input. |
| (d) `composing_units` with effective dates, or derived from relationships | **Roadmap**: C0493 and C0487 now list dated, qualified entries as text; `composing_units` now holds unit ids and `composing_text` the components that are not units (202 of 318 entries); a derived composing view needs formation-to-corps rows for every component (the combat commands and cavalry groups are not all units yet). Derive it from `command_relations.csv` once component coverage is complete. |
| (e) `last_confirmed_hq_location` | **Roadmap**: needs the temporal reconstruction layer. Until then Lesneven is documented as the 18 August headquarters observation and the last confirmed place before the CMH list's St Aubin on 24 September; the VIII Corps 5 and 10 September rows keep Brest-sector points and say so. |
| Explained-exception mechanism for the coordinate checker | **Done**: `data/coordinate_exceptions.csv` (speed warnings only so far; five entries with sources), and the speed check now measures days between the dates the coordinates belong to (`location_date`). Three speed warnings that this made visible remain open and unchecked: C0253, C0419, C0480. |

## Generated files at scale

The combined outputs in `build/` (`all_claims.csv`, `claims.geojson`) are committed today because the proof of concept is small. The per-unit files in `data/claims/` are the master copy; there are deliberately no monthly or per-theater exports. Once the dataset grows (for example the whole war), stop committing the generated combined files and publish them as release assets instead (build them in CI on each tagged release), so the repository history stays small and diffs stay readable. At that point remove `build/` from version control, add it to `.gitignore`, and drop the "build is up to date" check from the workflow.

## Operations table (26 October 2026)

Done: `data/operations.csv` (36 rows: the Overlord umbrella, 9 campaigns of which 6 are CMH streamers, 12 operations, 12 battles and 2 phases), `operation_relations.csv`, `operation_exceptions.csv`, `operation_id` on every claim, derived `campaign` / `operation_name` / `sub_operation_name` / `operation_path` in `build/all_claims.csv`, validator rules. Open points: the operation boundaries listed in [`review/operations_backfill.md`](review/operations_backfill.md) (Cobra placement, Overlord end date, Cherbourg window, Brittany and Brest end dates, derived Pursuit dates, Bulge end date, Aachen and Siegfried Line start dates); 20 claims left on a campaign because no operation row fits (Le Mans, Loire, Seine, Alsace, early Pursuit and Roer rows); rows for the Hedgerows, Hurtgen Forest, Colmar Pocket, Nordwind and the Central Europe operations once claims need them; a `claim_operations` link table only if a claim is found that belongs to two operations at once (none yet).

## Claim-to-reference links stored on claims (26 October 2026)

Done: `reference_ids` and `source_locator` on every claim (566 claims; 158 with a locator taken from URL page fragments), `data/claim_reference_roles.csv` (619 rows: 564 location, 21 strength, 20 command_relationship, 12 date, 2 event; every other link is `general`), `source_family` on every reference, validator checks (ids exist, one reference per claim, URL citations resolve to the stored ids, role rows match the links, two source families at confidence 0.9 as a warning) and `scripts/migrate_claim_references.py`. Next: add roles by hand while checking sources (only 70 notes name what a source supports, so most links are `general`); add page numbers for the 408 claims without a locator when a source is next read; consider promoting the 0.9 family rule to an error after the next batch, and a rule that confidence 0.7 needs a second family (115 + 240 claims at 0.7 and 0.5 currently rest on one non-geocoder family).

## Activity status pilot (29 October 2026): open questions

The design and the pilot are in `docs/activity_status_design.md`. These questions are open, each with a recommendation:

1. **A separate `rest` value?** Recommendation: no. The sources pair rest with refitting; keep `refitting`.
2. **Units leaving the theatre** (the 1st Airborne and the Polish brigade to England, I Canadian Corps from Italy). Recommendation: use `transit` start/end rows and close any open span at the departure.
3. **Mixed states** (the Poles at Moerdijk "rested and re-equipped" while holding the line, Stacey p. 435; one brigade in the line, two at rest). Recommendation: record the main body's state and add subordinate rows only where a source separates them.
4. **Spans left open in the pilot** (the 1st Airborne, the 50th, the 4th Canadian Armoured and the 3e DIA). Recommendation: close them in the full rollout where a source allows. The open-span "possible" reading stays as the fallback.
5. **Implicit ends.** Recommendation: keep them, but report them as inferred (0.3). Add an explicit end row whenever a source dates the end.
6. **Store the activity confidence, or derive it?** Recommendation: derive it, as the pilot does. Add the three columns to `build/claims.geojson` only after the geojson size problem is solved.
7. **The French divisions have no refit sources yet** (only a reserve point and an in_line start). Recommendation: use the de Lattre or Vigneras material in the French rollout.
8. **Full rollout.** Recommendation: about 80-95 hours in batches of about 15 divisions by nationality; divisions and fortresses first; corps, armies and brigades blank.

## Open design questions

- Per-day interpolation versus pure event rows (currently pure events).
- Whether a unit's primary `theater` should be derived from its claims once several theaters exist (currently set by hand in `units.csv`).
- Resolved (October 2026): the master time-dependent relations table is `data/command_relations.csv`; intervals are hand-maintained there, the claims are linked evidence. Open: whether `parent_unit_id` should one day be derived from the table (41 of 566 values are editorial) and the contradictory overlaps documented in `data/command_relation_overlaps.csv` (19), which need source review.

## Batch 2: command transitions, 25 July - 15 September 1944 (25 October 2026)

Done: First/Third/Ninth Army and 12th Army Group handovers (1 August; 1 September; Ninth Army 5 and 10 September), V, VII, VIII, XII, XV, XIX and XX Corps command posts and transitions, the 2nd Armored/XIX Corps sequence, Cobra division rows (1st, 2nd Armored, 3rd Armored), the 6th Armored and 83rd Divisions under Ninth Army, and a regeneration of every table-derived relationship from the CMH tables (army and army-group columns now `assigned`/`attached`/`operational_control`; corps stays `unspecified_subordination` except where a Green Book page says attached).

Still open after Batch 2:
- Seventh Army headquarters place on 15 September 1944 (not found; row C0296 stays an unlocated representative point at confidence 0.3).
- VIII Corps headquarters on 5 and 10 September: only the 18 August move to Lesneven is documented; those two rows keep Brest-sector points.
- First Army 1-2 August headquarters: Vouilly (monument: stayed until 2 August) and Saint-Gilles (CMH list from 1 August) are 23 km apart and are now modelled as a move in sequence (C0484, C0485; conflict group G015 dissolved in the Batch 2 corrections); the order of events within 1-2 August is not documented.
- The 83rd Division's brief simultaneous attachment to VIII and XV Corps (Breakout p428) is not given a row: the table shows XV Corps on 1 August and VIII Corps on 3 August and no source dates the overlap.
- Regiment-level Cobra rows (the 4th Division's 8th Infantry at La Chapelle-en-Juger, the 9th and 30th Divisions on 25 July) were not added: the pages read here do not give a place and date together.
- The corps and army lists from World War II by Day are a secondary compilation; replace them with the CMH ETO-OB corps and army pages if those become reachable.

## Batch 3: Oct-Dec 1944 milestones (25 October 2026)

Done: 20 December command change (12th Army Group keeps Third Army; First and Ninth Armies to 21st Army Group with explicit ends from the CMH tables); First, Third, Seventh and Ninth Army milestones; the Aachen link-up (16 October) and surrender (21 October); the Roer offensive start (16 November) for VII and XIX Corps; XIII Corps (8 and 24 November) as a newly operational corps, III Corps (4 December table date, 18 December decision) and XXI Corps (27 December table date) first listed over divisions; Lorraine (XII and XX Corps 8-9 November, 10th Armored), Seventh Army (XV Corps 13 November, 100th and 103rd Divisions' first combat); the Bulge (28th and 106th Divisions, VIII Corps headquarters move and handover to Third Army, 82nd Airborne, XVIII Airborne Corps, 101st Airborne at Bastogne (a sequence since the Batch 3 review; G016 dissolved), 4th Armored and its Combat Command Reserve at Assenois, III Corps counterattack, XII Corps ordered to Luxembourg, 84th and 75th Divisions).

Not covered (no rows were added; the sources for them were not read in this batch): V and VI Corps command-post places and XX Corps places other than 9 November; the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 9th and 30th Infantry Divisions after the dates already in the dataset; the 7th, 3rd and 9th Armored Divisions in the Bulge; the 9th, 11th, 12th and 14th Armored, 17th Airborne, 42nd, 78th, 95th and 99th Divisions (still placeholders); the Hurtgen Forest in detail (28th Division's Schmidt attack, 2 November, is not a row); the Colmar pocket beyond XXI Corps; Metz fall (22 November); Strasbourg (23 November); the French First Army and II Corps relationships to Seventh Army; the First Army headquarters move to Tongeren (atlas, 24 December). XVI, XXII and XXIII Corps and the Fifteenth Army have no source in the window (XVI Corps appears in the 75th Division table in December but has no Green Book or atlas entry before January). 13th Airborne, 13th, 16th and 20th Armored arrive in 1945; 8th Armored, 66th, 69th, 70th and 76th Divisions were in the United Kingdom or just landing.

Open points: the 101st / VIII Corps handover hour on 20 December (Green Book "during the 20th" against the CMH table's 17 December); the VIII Corps headquarters stays at Neufchâteau from 18 December while Middleton remains in Bastogne; First Army's own move to Chaudfontaine is dated 19 December (p423) while the atlas has no entry until Tongeren on 24 December; the XVIII Airborne Corps command post place is taken from the 82nd's.

## Batch 4: January to March 1945 milestones (26 October 2026)

Branch `batch4-jan-mar-1945` (not merged; no pull request). Scope approved by Scott on 26 October 2026: US Army ground units, army group to division level; a milestone pass with event-based rows, not a daily record; no combat commands; no German, British, Canadian or French claim subjects (they appear only as higher headquarters in relations).

Done: 68 claims, C0574 to C0641 (January 22, February 15, March 31), for 34 units; 19 placeholders became active (XVI Corps, Fifteenth Army, 17th Airborne; 8th, 9th, 11th, 12th and 14th Armored; 42nd, 63rd, 65th, 66th, 69th, 70th, 76th, 78th, 89th, 95th and 99th Infantry). Milestones: the end of the Bulge and Nordwind (January), the Colmar Pocket, Herrlisheim and the 12th Armored, the West Wall and the Roer dams, the Sauer and Saar crossings, Operation Grenade, Remagen (7 March), Trier (1 March), the Moselle and the Saar-Palatinate offensive, the Rhine crossings, and First, Ninth and Fifteenth Army and XII and XVI Corps command milestones. Command relations use the CMH ETO-OB assignment tables, with the table-start convention option C (V-013); 19 earlier relations were closed because the new claims show them ending (each carries a `Review` note and is listed in `docs/changelog.md`).

Not covered (no rows were added): the 13th, 16th and 20th Armored, 71st, 86th and 97th Infantry and XXII and XXIII Corps (still placeholders; the 13th Armored entered combat on 19 March and the 97th on 31 March, with no row yet); the 82nd Airborne and the 3rd Armored Division in 1945; most other divisions after the dates already in the dataset; headquarters moves of the armies and corps between milestones; combat commands, regiments and separate units; German, British, Canadian and French subjects. Table entries that the CMH tables list beyond 31 March 1945 are left open (each relation's note names the table end).

Open points for Scott: (1) the 65th Division was included because its first combat falls in the quarter; (2) the 89th Division's Moselle crossing date (conflict group G017: the preferred row is its 12 March first combat at Gillenfeld; the three disputed rows for 15, 16 and 17 March use a representative point at Cochem); (4) the 101st Airborne's relations after its 15 January claim stay open; (5) source disagreements kept as notes: the 78th Division (V Corps operational control in the Green Book, XVIII Airborne Corps in the table) and the 95th Division (XIX Corps attached in the Green Book, XIII Corps operational control in the table). Items resolved since were removed in clean-up 2 (29 October 2026); the others keep their numbers.

## Batch 5: April to 8 May 1945 milestones (27 October 2026)

Branch `batch5-apr-may-1945` (not merged; no pull request). Scope approved by Scott on 26 October 2026: US Army ground units, army group to division level; a milestone pass with event-based rows, not a daily record; no combat commands, regiments, or German, British, Canadian or French claim subjects (they appear only as higher headquarters in relations).

Done: 66 claims, C0647 to C0712 (1 to 14 April 17, 15 to 30 April 31, 1 to 8 May 18), for 50 units (44 already active; 6 became active: 13th, 16th and 20th Armored; 71st, 86th and 97th Infantry; each confirmed against its CMH ETO-OB table, R561, R562 and R571 to R574; no other US unit was a placeholder). Confidence: 3 rows at 0.9 (69th Division at Strehla, 25 April: The Last Offensive and Wikipedia, Elbe Day; 3rd Division at Nuremberg, 20 April: The Last Offensive and Wikipedia; 16th Armored Division at Pilsen, 6 May: The Last Offensive with the CMH list, and Wikipedia), 45 at 0.7, 18 at 0.5. Milestones: the Ruhr pocket (95th at Beckum, 8th at Hattingen, 95th at Dortmund, 13th Armored, 97th and 86th Infantry on the Sieg and at Attendorn, the Panzer Lehr surrender to the 99th Infantry), Ninth, First and Third Army headquarters; Ohrdruf (4th Armored) and Nordhausen (3rd Armored, 104th Infantry); the Elbe at Westerhausen, Tangermünde and Barby; Magdeburg, Halle, Colditz, Leipzig and the Mulde at Dessau; the Harz (1st Infantry on the Brocken, 9th at Wippra); Strehla on 25 April (the link-up with Soviet troops; Torgau itself has no row); Nuremberg; the Danube at Dillingen, Ingolstadt and Regensburg; Munich (45th, 20th Armored, 42nd at Dachau, 3rd at Haar for the Army Group G surrender); the Alps (10th Armored at Garmisch-Partenkirchen, 103rd at Innsbruck, 44th at Landeck, 101st Airborne at Miesbach); Austria (13th Armored at Tann, 71st at Sierning, 80th at Vöcklabruck, 11th Armored at Kirchschlag, 20th Armored at Prien, 86th at Burghausen); Czechoslovakia (97th at Tachov, 16th Armored at Pilsen); the Twenty-first Army and Schwerin (82nd Airborne at Ludwigslust, 8th at Schwerin); and corps headquarters (V, VI, VII, XII, XIII, XV, XIX, XX, XXI), the Seventh Army and the 6th Army Group. Three operations were added (`OP-RUHR-POCKET`, `OP-HARZ-POCKET`, `OP-NUREMBERG`); the other rows use the campaign `OP-CAMP-CENTRAL-EUROPE`. Country codes `AT` and `CZ` were added to the places vocabulary (`schema/vocabularies.json`).

Command relations: 80 new (CR0479 to CR0558) from the CMH ETO-OB assignment tables with the table-start convention option C (V-013); 41 earlier relations were closed because the table lists them ending (each carries a `Review` note with old, new and reason, and is listed in `docs/changelog.md`). Deferred items done: `CR0477` (95th to XXII Corps) closed on 1945-04-01 by the table-end rule; the 95th Division's relations to XIX Corps and the Ninth Army from 2 April, and to XVI Corps from 9 April (CR0479 to CR0481, CR0502). One expectation of `scripts/semantic_tests.py` changed (scenario 3, the 84th Division's VII Corps relation is now closed on 1945-01-22, with the reason in the comment).

**What Batch 5 does NOT cover:**
- Units and echelons: no combat commands, regiments, battalions, cavalry groups or corps troops; no German, British, Canadian, French or Soviet claim subjects. No April or May row for the 12th Army Group, the Fifteenth Army, the First Allied Airborne Army, III, VIII, XVI, XVIII Airborne, XXII or XXIII Corps (no source read dates a place; the Fifteenth Army History passages for XXII and XXIII Corps listed in the corps section above were not used), or for XIV and X Corps (unit records US-COR-014C and US-COR-010C were added on 27 October 2026; the CMH tables list them for the 95th and 4th Armored Divisions only briefly, the sources read place both corps in the Pacific, and no relation to them is recorded).
- Divisions already active but without an April or May row: 17th Airborne; 6th, 7th and 8th Armored; 4th, 5th, 26th, 28th, 29th, 35th, 36th, 63rd, 66th, 70th, 75th, 76th, 78th, 79th, 87th, 89th, 90th, 94th, 102nd and 106th Infantry. No source was read for their April and May milestones in this batch (for example the 6th Armored Division at Buchenwald, or the divisions that reduced the Ruhr pocket without a row here). 65th Infantry Division: no row for Linz or the Enns (no source for the date was found in The Last Offensive pages read), so its April rows are Regensburg only.
- Events inside a milestone pass: Torgau itself and the Soviet link-up on the 26th and 27th (only Strehla on 25 April), the Ruhr pocket's daily reduction (only a few division rows and the operation), the Rhine and Danube bridgeheads in detail, Leipzig and Halle after the first entry, Munich (only approaches and Dachau), Salzburg, Linz, Berchtesgaden, the Brenner link-up with Fifth Army (not Western Front), Czech Pilsen's surroundings, and the 7 and 8 May surrenders other than those named (Army Group G at Haar, Twenty-first Army at Ludwigslust, the 8 May cut-off rows). Camp liberations appear only as unit position milestones (Ohrdruf, Nordhausen, Dachau); no row for Buchenwald, Mauthausen, Flossenbürg, Langenstein or other camps.
- Relations: a table-based relation is recorded only for a table interval that covers the date of one of the unit's Batch 5 claims, so a period between two claims that the table lists (for example the 97th Division's Third Army and XII Corps entries of 19 to 27 April, or the 2nd Division's VII Corps entry of 28 to 30 April, both seen in the Combat Chronicles) has no relation; units without a Batch 5 claim keep the relations they had (open where no source dates the end); table entries that run past 8 May are left open and named in the note of each relation; units without a table (none in this batch) would have no relation; the relations of the 97th (CR0474), 86th (CR0476) and 82nd Airborne (CR0478) to XXII Corps were closed on their table end dates.
- Data fields: no commanders table, no `event_support` or `position` columns, no F-004 or F-005 work, no outside-review questions, no licence changes.

Open points for Scott: (1) the CMH ETO-OB pages of the 5th Armored Division (a row dated "31 Jun 44") and the 44th Infantry Division (a command-post date "18 Arp 45") contain typing errors; the reader skips the one row and takes the other as April, each stated in the note of the claim; (4) XXII and XXIII Corps still have no April or May rows; (5) the 71st Division's Danube crossing date ("the following night" after 24 April) is ambiguous and only the 25 April command post is recorded; (6) the Dachau date for the 42nd Division is 29 April by Wikipedia only (the Green Book gives no day), so the row is 0.7, not 0.9. Still open from this review: the table entries X and XIV are not confirmed by any source read (see the changelog). Items resolved since were removed in clean-up 2 (29 October 2026); the others keep their numbers.

## German Batch 1: German units, 30 June to 31 August 1944 (27 October 2026)

Branch `german-batch1-jul-aug-1944` (not merged; no pull request). Scope approved by Scott on 27 October 2026 (he agreed to start with 30 June to 31 August 1944): German units on the Western Front at army group, army, panzer group, corps and division level; Kampfgruppen only if already in the dataset; no regiments or combat commands. Source: the CMH Green Book Breakout and Pursuit (one source family, printed page numbers), plus Wikipedia as a second family for the command changes of OB West, Army Group B, Panzer Group West and the Fifth Panzer Army.

Done: 52 claims, `C0713` to `C0764` (July 16, August 36; confidence 0.3 for 9 representative-point rows, 0.5 for 34, 0.7 for 9; none 0.9 because every place rests on one family), for 30 units. Placeholders activated: Fifth Panzer Army (`GER-PZA-005A`), LXXXI Corps, LVIII Panzer Corps, the 9th Panzer, 116th Panzer, 1st SS Panzer, 2nd SS Panzer, 84th, 277th, 353rd, 363rd, 708th, 5th Parachute and 275th Divisions; one new unit record, `GER-PZG-EBERB` (Panzer Group Eberbach, an army-level headquarters formed for the second Avranches attack). 27 command relations (`CR0559` to `CR0585`); five earlier relations closed with inferred ends (`CR0039`, `CR0043`, `CR0054`, `CR0012`, `CR0046`, all `change_undated`); 15 places (`PL0510` to `PL0524`); 7 references (`R586` to `R592`); 3 conflict groups; a sixth semantic scenario (79 expectations).

Contents: the higher-headquarters changes (OB West 2 July, Army Group B 17 July and Model's takeover, Panzer Group West to Eberbach and its renaming as the Fifth Panzer Army, Seventh Army 31 July to 20 August); the German order of battle on 24 July; Panzer Lehr on 11 and 25 July; the LXXXIV Corps command change of 28 July; the Mortain counterattack (6 to 7 August) and the second Avranches plan (XLVII, LVIII Panzer Corps and LXXXI Corps, 10 to 12 August); the Falaise pocket (17 to 20 August: the II Parachute Corps, XLVII Panzer Corps, LXXXIV Corps, and the 2nd Panzer, 116th Panzer, 1st SS, 12th SS, 353rd, 277th, 84th and 363rd Divisions).

**What German Batch 1 does NOT cover:**
- 30 June to 24 July for most German units (Operation Goodwood and the British fighting near Caen, the Vire sector in July, the 2nd and 12th SS and 21st Panzer Divisions on the Caen front), and 1 to 31 August beyond the points above: the Totalize and Tractable fronts (I SS Panzer Corps, LXXXVI Corps, 85th, 89th, 272nd, 271st Divisions, 12th SS at Falaise before 17 August), the II SS Panzer Corps and 9th and 10th SS Panzer Divisions (rows need a decision on their parent after Panzer Group West was renamed), the Brittany and Brest garrisons (2nd Parachute, 266th, 343rd Divisions), 3rd Parachute Division before 20 August, the 17th SS and 21st Panzer Divisions after June, the Seine crossings and the retreat after 21 August. British operations (Goodwood, Bluecoat, Totalize, Tractable) are not in the operations table, so no row uses them.
- Regiments and combat commands; German air and naval units.

Open points for Scott: (1) the date of Model's takeover of Army Group B (19 August, inferred from the Green Book, against 17 August in Wikipedia; conflict group `G018`, both rows disputed); (2) the date of the renaming of Panzer Group West as the Fifth Panzer Army (5 August in the Green Book, 1 August in Wikipedia; `G020`) and of Eberbach's takeover from Geyr (4 July against 2 July; `G019`); (3) who took over the Seventh Army when Hausser was wounded on 20 August (Funck, ch. 27 p. 557; Eberbach, ch. 31 p. 663 and Wikipedia): left in the note of `C0725`; (5) Panzer Group West and the Fifth Panzer Army stay two unit records (lineage rename), so a chain through the I SS Panzer Corps still names Panzer Group West after 5 August; (6) the 12th SS Panzer Division stays under the I SS Panzer Corps (`possible`) until 19 August because no source read dates its move to the LXXXIV Corps. Items resolved since were removed in clean-up 2 (29 October 2026); the others keep their numbers.

## German Batch 1b: German units, 30 June to 24 July 1944 (27 October 2026)

Branch `german-batch1b-jul-1944`, built on the unmerged `german-batch1-jul-aug-1944` (compare it with that branch, not with `main`). Scope approved by Scott on 27 October 2026 (including low-confidence rows: "Can you include claims from these source with low ratings or does that not make sense?"). German units at division level and above, 30 June to 24 July 1944; Kampfgruppen only if already in the dataset. Sources: the CMH Green Books (one family), the Lexikon der Wehrmacht, the Nafziger Collection file 944BGAA (an order of battle of July 1944 with footnotes; it is of the Tessin lineage and counts as the same family as the Lexikon and Feldgrau), Feldgrau.com for the Panzer Lehr date, Schramm's OKW war diary (Band IV/1, archive.org OCR; a second family) for OB West, Panzer Group West and Army Group B, and Wikipedia for the dates of the three operations only.

Done: 57 claims, `C0765` to `C0821` (confidence 0.3 for 19 rows, 0.5 for 28 and 0.7 for 10; none higher, because every place rests on one family or on an area), for 28 units, 4 of them newly active (LXXXVI Corps, 89th, 271st and 272nd Infantry Divisions). Command level: Kluge takes OB West at 2000 hours on 3 July, Eberbach takes Panzer Group West on 2 July (alternate row in `G019`), Kluge leads Army Group B from 19 July (alternate row in `G021`; Schramm). Corps: the line of corps on 1 July (LXXXVI, I SS, II SS, XLVII), the LXXXVI Corps on 9 and 18 July, the LXXXIV Corps withdrawal of 13/14 July, and the Nafziger July lists for the I SS, II SS and LXXXVI Corps. Divisions: 1st, 2nd, 9th, 10th and 12th SS, 17th SS, 21st Panzer, Panzer Lehr, 91st, 243rd, 352nd, 353rd, 3rd Parachute, 271st, 272nd, and month-level rows for the 346th, 711th, 716th, 89th and 116th Panzer Divisions. Operations: `OP-CHARNWOOD`, `OP-JUPITER` and `OP-GOODWOOD` (German-side context, dates from Wikipedia). 8 command relations (`CR0586` to `CR0593`); four earlier relations moved on the day-level evidence (`CR0581` start 4 July, `CR0046` end 3 July, `CR0584` start 11 July, `CR0012` end 10 July) and two had their inferred end date updated (`CR0043`, `CR0054`); `CR0039` was repaired; 15 places, 12 references, 5 conflict groups (`G021` Army Group B on 17 or 19 July; `G022` the month Panzer Lehr came under the LXXXIV Corps; `G023` the corps of the 12th SS in July; `G024` the 716th Division in southern France or Normandy; `G025` the 711th Division in the LXXXI or LXXXVI Corps). A seventh semantic scenario (12 expectations) and replaced expectations in scenario 6.

Month-level rows (the source gives only "July" or an area) carry 15 July as an approximate date, confidence 0.3 and a note that says so; they never invent a point (a representative point with a precision of 25 or 50 km, `location_basis` `representative_point` or `operational_area`).

**What German Batch 1b does NOT cover:**
- 25 July onwards (Cobra) is Batch 1; `OP-COBRA` and `OP-EPSOM` exist but no German-side rows were added for them; no row for 26 June to 1 July beyond the 1 July corps line.
- No July source was read for the 85th Division (the Lexikon table has May, then August); the 91st, 243rd and 353rd have one row each in July before 24 July; the 116th Panzer Division has only a month-level row; the 326th Division and the 16th Luftwaffe Field Division are named in the Nafziger list but have no rows (16th is in `composing_units` of the LXXXVI Corps row only).
- Schramm's remark on the burn-out of units on 1 July (p. 323) was not made into claims.
- Regiments, Kampfgruppen not already in the dataset, and air and naval units.

Open points for Scott: (1) `G021`, `G023` and `G025`: no winner chosen; each needs a source outside those read (`G022` and `G024` were settled on 28 October 2026). (3) The parent of the II SS Panzer Corps and the 9th and 10th SS Panzer Divisions (`CR0051`, `CR0052`) and of the 271st Division: Nafziger and the Lexikon table support the II SS Panzer Corps for July; nothing was changed. (4) The 21st Panzer Division: `CR0038` (under the I SS Panzer Corps) is still open; Nafziger has it in the LXXXVI Corps, the Lexikon "z. Vfg. Panzergruppe West". (5) `CR0011` and `CR0014` (346th and 711th Divisions under the Fifteenth Army) are not closed although the July rows put them in corps of Panzer Group West. (6) The Lexikon 12th SS page has a second "11. Juli" passage about Dreux that cannot be July 1944 (noted in `C0778`), and the 271st page gives Bernay as 130 km from Lisieux (it is about 35 km): neither fact was used. (7) `build/all_claims.csv` on Drive is stale. Items resolved since were removed in clean-up 2 (29 October 2026); the others keep their numbers.

## German Batch 2: German units, 1 September to 31 October 1944 (27 October 2026)

Branch `german-batch2-sep-oct-1944`, branched from `main` (`5da3a3c`, which holds German Batch 1 and 1b after PR #19; compare it with `main`). Scope approved by Scott on 27 October 2026 ("Sounds good. Let's to batch 2"): German units at division level and above, 1 September to 31 October 1944; Panzer brigades and Panzer groups only if they meet the division-equivalent rule (none do; brigades are skipped). Sources: CMH Green Books (Siegfried Line, Lorraine and Riviera-to-the-Rhine campaign volumes; one family), the Schramm OKW war diary (one family), the Lexikon der Wehrmacht division and corps tables (one family), Wikipedia for operation dates and some coordinates, OpenStreetMap Nominatim for coordinates. No Nafziger T-312, T-311 or T-410 file was used for a row (the pages were fetched; see "does NOT cover").

Done: 111 claims, `C0822` to `C0932` (confidence 0.3 for 54 rows, 0.5 for 49, 0.7 for 8), for 61 units; 33 placeholders became active: Army Group G, First Parachute Army, First, Fifteenth and Nineteenth Armies, LXIV, LXVI, LXXIV, LXXXII, LXXXVIII, XIII SS and XII SS Corps, IV Luftwaffe Field Corps, the 3rd Panzer Grenadier, 11th Panzer, 15th Panzer Grenadier, 12th, 183rd, 246th, 553rd and 559th Volksgrenadier divisions, the 48th, 49th, 59th, 64th, 70th, 159th, 198th, 245th, 347th, 462nd and 719th Infantry Divisions and the 6th Fallschirmjäger Division. Command level: OB West (Rundstedt reappointed 1 September, assumed command 5 September, Bad Kreuznach on 29 September), Army Group B (Model at Oosterbeek on 17 September), Army Group G (Blaskowitz to Balck, with the 18 and 21 September dates both kept as event definitions), the First Parachute Army (4 September, Vught on 17 September, 23 September order), First, Fifth Panzer, Seventh, Fifteenth and Nineteenth Armies. Corps: XLVII and LVIII Panzer, XIII SS, LXXXI, II SS Panzer, II Parachute, LXXXVIII, LXVI, LXIV and IV Luftwaffe Field Corps, plus Lexikon month-level rows. Divisions: the Aachen defence (116th Panzer, 9th Panzer, 12th, 183rd, 49th, 275th, 353rd and 246th), Lorraine (3rd and 15th Panzer Grenadier, 17th SS, 21st and 11th Panzer, 553rd, 559th, 462nd), the Netherlands (9th and 10th SS, 59th, 70th, 64th, 245th, Fallschirm divisions 3, 5, 6) and Lexikon month-level rows for the 2nd and 2nd SS Panzer, Panzer Lehr, 1st and 12th SS, 719th, 198th, 716th, 159th, 48th, 89th, 84th and others.

Month-level and inferred rows carry the 15th (or the stated "by" date), confidence 0.3 and a note that says so. 24 earlier relations were closed as inferred (listed in `docs/changelog.md`); `CR0038` (21st Panzer Division) and `CR0561` (Seventh Army under the Fifth Panzer Army) were settled by sources read in this batch; the First Parachute Army to Army Group B relation was added.

**Source finding that differs from the task text:** "Army Group B (Model to 14 Sep)" is not supported. Lexikon der Wehrmacht has Model commanding Army Group B from 17 August 1944 to the capitulation; CMH has him at Oosterbeek on 17 September and issuing orders on 23 September (ch. 7 and 8). He gave up OB West only (Rundstedt reappointed 1 September, assumed command 5 September, Schramm and Lexikon). `C0825` records Model with Army Group B on 17 September; no row says he left it on 14 September.

**What German Batch 2 does NOT cover:**
- Panzer brigades (the 106th, 112th and 113th are named only in notes), Panzer groups and Kampfgruppen not already in the dataset; regiments; air and naval units.
- The Nafziger T-312, T-311 and T-410 order-of-battle files (the pages were fetched; no row was written from them), so every row rests on CMH, Schramm or the Lexikon table; no row has an independent third family.
- Lexikon rows left out because the table names only a region for the month: the 346th, 711th, 712th, 85th (September), 226th, 331st and 344th Divisions ("Belgien", "Niederlande", "Dünkirchen"), the 70th Division in October ("Gent"), the 9th Panzer, 89th, 49th and 59th Divisions in September (a region or a parent that differs from CMH), the II Parachute Corps in October (the table names only "Niederlande").
- Rows after 27 September for the Lorraine divisions that have a CMH row earlier (3rd and 15th Panzer Grenadier, 17th SS, 21st Panzer, 553rd) and after 17 September for the Aachen divisions other than the 246th; the Metz forts and the fall of Metz (`OP-METZ` exists as a context record; no German row is dated inside it).
- The LXXX Corps and the LXXXV Corps (no row of their own, so the chain of Panzer Lehr and the 353rd Division stops at LXXX Corps), Corps Feldt and Division von Tettau.
- No German-side record was added for Market Garden, Aachen or the Scheldt beyond the rows' `operation_id`.
- 1 to 3 September in the Seine retreat (other than OB West on 1 September).

Open points for Scott: (1) `G021`, `G023` and `G025`: no source read in this batch settles them; left as in Batch 1b (`G022`, `G024` and the 243rd Division were settled on 28 October 2026). (2) The parent of the II SS Panzer Corps and the 9th and 10th SS (`CR0051`, `CR0052`): the CMH shows both divisions in the II SS Panzer Corps on 17 September, so the rows are linked to the existing relations; the Lexikon table's "Auffrischung, Eifel" for the corps in September contradicts CMH (Arnhem) and was not written as a row. (3) `CR0011` and `CR0014` (346th and 711th Divisions under the Fifteenth Army): the Lexikon September rows name only "Belgien" and "Niederlande" and were not written. (4) The Army Group G command change (Blaskowitz to Balck): Schramm has 18 September, CMH and the Lexikon 21 September; both are kept as event definitions in `C0832` and `C0833`, no group. (5) The LXXX Corps and the LXXXV Corps have no parent relation. (6) The XLVII Panzer Corps on 4 September (ordered to Mirecourt) against 5 September (holding the Moselle line): kept as two rows. (7) Rows whose date is inferred (the 353rd Division 17 September, the 246th Division 12 October) start their relation on the row date; the answer can read "certain" on that day.

## German Batch 2 follow-up: Model / Army Group B and the LXXX Corps (27 October 2026)

Branch `german-batch2-followup-model-lxxx`, cut from `german-batch2-sep-oct-1944`. Compare it with `german-batch2-sep-oct-1944` (against `main` it also shows Batch 2). After Batch 2 is merged into `main` the follow-up can be merged normally. Scope approved by Scott on 27 October 2026 ("Yes") from a research note whose facts were treated as unverified until each cited page was read again; only what could be read was recorded. Sources: CMH Green Books (one family: Breakout and Pursuit, Siegfried Line, Ardennes, The Last Offensive, Lorraine), the Schramm OKW war diary (Model's arrival), the Lexikon der Wehrmacht (Army Group B and LXXX Corps pages; one family together with the Tessin and Bundesarchiv listing), English and German Wikipedia (one family), the Nafziger Collection files of 16 and 26 November 1944 (a separate family: they draw on the OKW war diary), EHRI, HistoryNet, battle-of-arnhem.com (Internet Archive copy) and Konfliktlandschaften (Internet Archive copy).

Done: 22 claims, `C0933` to `C0954` (confidence 0.7 for 2, 0.5 for 14, 0.3 for 6; rubric by source family: 0.7 two families on the day and place, 0.5 one family, 0.3 an approximate date or place or one popular source). **Army Group B** (`C0933` to `C0937`): Model arrives at La Roche-Guyon on 17 August 1944, the headquarters at Oosterbeek on 14 September, Fichtenhain on 27 October, Wuppertal on 15 April 1945 and Haan on 17 April 1945 with the army group disestablished (`disestablished` true, `destroyed` false). `CR0004` (Army Group B under OB West) is closed on 17 April 1945. **LXXX Corps** (`GER-COR-080C`, activated; `C0938` to `C0944`, `C0946` to `C0949`): the corps under the First Army from 5 September 1944 (Wallendorf on 14 and 16 September), under the Seventh Army from 18 September 1944 (`CR0667`), back under the First Army from 21 March 1945 (`CR0669`) and under the Nineteenth Army from 19 April 1945 (`CR0670`); the Seventh Army moves from Army Group B to Army Group G on 2 March 1945 (`C0945`, `CR0668`; `CR0005` closed on 1 March). The English Wikipedia's "October" for the move to the Seventh Army is the alternate row `C0942` in the new group `G026` (`C0941`, 18 September, preferred). Divisions: Panzer Lehr (`C0950`) and the 5th Fallschirmjäger (`C0951`, 16 September; `CR0630` closed on 15 September), the 353rd (`C0952`, 26 November, 0.3), the 212th Volksgrenadier (`C0953`) and 276th Volksgrenadier (`C0954`) Divisions on 26 November and 16 December.

**Source findings.** (a) No claim, here or earlier, says Model's Army Group B command ended on 14 September: the data never had it (see "German Batch 2" above); notes were added to `C0715`, `C0716` and `C0822`. (b) Model's command of Army Group B starts on 17 August (`C0716`: Lexikon, Bundesarchiv listing, Wikipedia); the CMH wording "commander-designate until 18/19 August" (`C0715`) stays in group `G018` with no preferred row. (c) Model's death on 21 April 1945 is a person event and is only in the text of `C0937`. (d) The end of Model's OB West stint is 4/5 September (CMH Breakout ch. 32 p. 700; English Wikipedia "eighteen days"); the German Wikipedia says 3 September; this is a note on `C0823`, not a row.

**What the follow-up does NOT cover:** no LXXX Corps headquarters place (no source read names one; the rows are sector points and say so); LXXX Corps rows between 13 October and 26 November 1944, between 16 December 1944 and 2 March 1945 and between 2 April and 19 April 1945 (the rows are milestones); a row for the Nineteenth Army or the First Army themselves; the later movements of the LXXX Corps divisions; the rejected items listed in `docs/changelog.md`.

Open points for Scott: (1) `G018` (17 against 18/19 August) and `G026` (18 September against October) stay open by design. (3) Wuppertal (15 April) and Haan (17 April) are at 0.3; the headquarters of Army Group B in April 1945 rests on one popular source (HistoryNet) for the place. (4) The grid shows many chains "possible" to "certain" through the Army Group B to OB West relation: this is intended (new dated listings). Items resolved since were removed in clean-up 2 (29 October 2026); the others keep their numbers.


## German 1945 Batch 1: German units, 1 January to 31 March 1945 (28 October 2026)

Branch `german-1945-batch1-jan-mar` (branched from `main` `85c55ee`, PR #23). Scope approved by Scott: German units at division level and above on the Western Front, 1 January to 31 March 1945, same method, sources, confidence rubric and low-confidence allowance as German Batches 2 and 3. Sources: CMH Green Books (The Last Offensive, Riviera to the Rhine, The Ardennes; one family), the Lexikon der Wehrmacht tables and commander lists, the Nafziger Collection where read earlier, OpenStreetMap Nominatim for coordinates only. No Feldgrau, no German Wikipedia as a sole source.

Done: 119 claims, `C1076` to `C1194` (confidence 0.7 for 10 rows, 0.5 for 71, 0.3 for 38), for 76 units, dated 1 January to 28 March 1945 (January 57, February 25, March 37). Six placeholders became active (Army Group Oberrhein, XIV SS Corps, 2nd, 7th and 8th Fallschirmjäger Divisions, 416th Infantry Division). Command level: OB West (14 January, Kesselring replaces Rundstedt on 10 March), Army Groups B, G, H and Oberrhein (Himmler's Nordwind attack, Hausser from 22 January, the dissolution on 29 January, Blaskowitz to Army Group H on 28 January), the First, Fifth Panzer, Sixth Panzer, Seventh, Fifteenth, Nineteenth, Twenty-fifth and First Parachute Armies with their command changes (Manteuffel to Harpe, Brandenberger to Felber to Obstfelder, Rasp to Foertsch to Obstfelder to Brandenberger, Schlemm to Blumentritt). Corps and divisions: the withdrawal from the Bulge, Nordwind (Gambsheim, Hatten, Rittershoffen, Lauterbourg), the Colmar Pocket order of battle, February Lexikon listings, the Eifel, the Remagen bridgehead (contingents opposite it on 17 March), LXXXIX Corps at Koblenz and the Seventh Army collapse, the Rhine crossing at Wesel against the 116th Panzer Division and the First Parachute Army. Places: 12 new (`PL0627` to `PL0638`). References: 4 new (`R656` to `R659`). Relations: 59 new (`CR0757` to `CR0815`); 42 earlier relations closed as inferred (listed in `docs/changelog.md`). One conflict group, `G027` (First Parachute Army, Blumentritt's arrival, 20 against 28 March). Semantic scenario 11 (15 expectations). The month-level February rows (Lexikon table, no day) are at 0.3 with the 15th as an approximate date.

**What German 1945 Batch 1 does NOT cover:**
- Army Group Vistula (Eastern Front); April to May 1945 (German 1945 Batch 2): Schulz's appointment to Army Group G (5 April) and Busch's to Army Group H (6 April) are not recorded.
- The Panzer brigades (`PZB-105` to `PZB-150`) and the 30th SS Division (Scott's decision), and the 6th SS Mountain Division Nord (placeholder; the sources read give only a passing mention, so no claim) and the 2nd Mountain Division (no unit row).
- Eastern-Front-bound divisions (269th, 338th, 712th and others) beyond their western rows; the 8 February withdrawal from the Colmar Pocket is not written as its own row.
- Operations: `OP-VERITABLE` does not exist; the February Roer and Reichswald rows carry `OP-CAMP-RHINELAND`, the March rows `OP-REMAGEN`, `OP-GRENADE` or `OP-CAMP-CENTRAL-EUROPE`. Adding it is an open point.
- Divisions in the Remagen and Eifel fighting that the sources read name only in a list are given one row at 0.5, not one per day; the Lexikon table rows without a day are at 0.3.

**Open points (my recommendation for each):**
1. `CR0688` (LXVII Corps under the Sixth Panzer Army from 16 December): CMH Last Offensive ch. 2 p. 45-46 puts the corps under the Fifteenth Army until the transfer on 17 January; Nafziger and the Lexikon say the Sixth Panzer Army on 15 December. Recommendation: keep the relation and the conflict in the notes; do not move the start date without a third source.
2. `CR0687` (Sixth Panzer Army under Army Group B) stays open; the Lexikon table gives 21 January for the move to Hungary under the Replacement Army/OKH, CMH the order of 22 January. Recommendation: close it only if Scott wants the Hungary move recorded as a command change (it is a different theatre).
3. Blumentritt takes the First Parachute Army on 28 March (CMH, preferred) or 20 March (Lexikon): group `G027`. Recommendation: keep both rows.
4. The Lexikon's Twenty-fifth Army list (Blumentritt until 21 March, Blaskowitz from 21 March, Kleffel from 30 March) against CMH (Blumentritt leaves on 28 March): `C1194` states both and is read together with `G027`. Recommendation: keep.
5. Manteuffel's departure from the Fifth Panzer Army: CMH says 8 March, the Lexikon gives 9 March for Harpe; one claim (`C1186`) states both dates (one day apart, so no conflict group). The 1 March exchange of zones between the Fifth Panzer and Fifteenth Armies is `C1158`. Recommendation: keep.
6. Army Group Oberrhein ends on 22/23 January (Hausser) in CMH and on 29 January in the Lexikon; both dates are in separate rows (`C1124` and `C1125`; the dates differ by days, so they are separate rows, not a conflict group); keep.
7. `OP-VERITABLE`: recommend adding it with the next batch only if Scott wants the Reichswald rows moved off `OP-CAMP-RHINELAND`.

## German 1945 Batch 2: German units, 1 April to 8 May 1945 (28 October 2026)

Branch `german-1945-batch2-apr-may` (branched from `main` `6d6bf99`, PR #24). Scope approved by Scott ("Go ahead and start the next batch", 28 October 2026): German units at division level and above on the Western Front, 1 April to 8 May 1945, same method, sources, confidence rubric and low-confidence allowance as the earlier German batches. Sources: CMH Green Books (The Last Offensive ch. 14 to 20; one family), the Lexikon der Wehrmacht tables, commander lists and page texts (Tessin; one family), the Avalon Project texts of the Lüneburg and Berlin instruments, English Wikipedia for the Lüneburg Heath place only, OpenStreetMap Nominatim for coordinates only. No Feldgrau, no German Wikipedia as a sole source. Where a place is approximate the row uses the approximate place (a town point or a labelled representative point), never an invented exact point.

Done: 83 claims, `C1195` to `C1277` (confidence 0.9 for 2 rows, 0.7 for 13, 0.5 for 33, 0.3 for 35), for 53 units, dated 1 April to 8 May 1945 (April 69, May 14). Six placeholders became active and six units were added (listed in `docs/changelog.md`). Command level: Army Group B encircled in the Ruhr pocket on 1 April, its armies' surrenders (Fifteenth Army 13 April, Fifth Panzer Army 17 April, the rest on 18 April; Model's dissolution order and `C0937` are unchanged), OB West (Kesselring, the move south on 10 April, the structure of 24 and 25 April), Army Group G (Schulz from 5 April, the Lexikon's 29 April dissolution order, the surrender at Haar on 5 May), Army Group H becoming OB Nordwest under Busch on 6 and 7 April and its surrender at Lüneburg Heath on 4 May, the First Army (Würzburg on 3 April, Haar on 5 May), the Seventh Army (under OB West from 28 April, Karlsbad on 8 May), the Eleventh Army (Hitzfeld 2 April, Lucht 8 April, captured near Blankenburg 23 April), the Twelfth Army (activated 10 April, Wenck at Roßlau on 12 April, the Dessau bridgehead, the 4 May talks with the US Ninth Army), the Nineteenth Army (Lake Constance 21 April, Innsbruck 5 May), the Twenty-fourth Army, the Twenty-fifth Army, the First Parachute Army (Student 10 April, Straube 28 April, Oldenburg 5 May), Army Blumentritt (Bremen to 26 April), and OKW (Reims 7 May, Berlin 8 May). Corps and divisions: the Ruhr perimeter on 1 April (Gruppe von Lüttwitz, LIII Corps at Winterberg, Panzer Lehr at Hallenberg, the 176th and 3rd Panzergrenadier Divisions), the 15 to 18 April surrenders, the Harz and Elbe divisions (Clausewitz, Scharnhorst, Hutten), Nuremberg (17th SS Panzergrenadier and 2nd Mountain Divisions), the Black Forest (XVIII SS Corps, LXIV Corps), the 11th Panzer Division's surrender on 4 May, the XIII SS and LXXXII Corps surrender arrangements of 8 May, and 17 Lexikon April table rows (one per division or corps, 0.3 with the 15th as an approximate date, or 0.5 where the row carries a day).

Earlier relations: 27 were closed (3 at a sourced date, 6 at the end of resistance in the Ruhr pocket on 18 April, 18 inferred from the next claim; listed with old value, new value and reason in `docs/changelog.md`). 15 of them rest on Lexikon April table rows; see open point 2. One existing claim (`C0937`) had a format typo corrected.

**What German 1945 Batch 2 does NOT cover:**
- The Panzer brigades (for example Panzer Brigade Thüringen) and the 30th SS Division (Scott's decision), Division Nibelungen, Division Potsdam, Divisions Theodor Körner, Friedrich Ludwig Jahn and Ferdinand von Schill.
- The XII Corps (Osterkamp) and XIII Corps (Bork) in the Seventh and First Army areas, the LXIII and XC Corps in April, the LXXXIX Corps and Group Hoehne's surrender (undated in CMH), the LXXXV Corps order of 26 April, Kesselring's departure from Ohrdruf, and most of the First Army's divisions between Würzburg and the Danube.
- Divisions of the Alpine and Austrian area after 5 May; individual divisions in the Ruhr pocket other than those named, the 176th Division after 1 April (the Lexikon puts it at Duisburg in April, CMH at Winterberg on 1 April; no row), the 89th Division ("in Umgliederung") and the 719th Division ("Verbleib unbekannt").
- Hamburg, Bremen and Holland below army level; Norway, Denmark and Italy; Army Group Vistula, the Ninth Army, the Twelfth Army's operations east of the Elbe after 21 April and units shown in the East.
- Operations: no operation added; German rows sit under `OP-RUHR-POCKET`, `OP-HARZ-POCKET`, `OP-NUREMBERG` and `OP-CAMP-CENTRAL-EUROPE`. `OP-VERITABLE` is still missing.
- The answer grid (`wc_grid.py`) runs only to 5 March 1945, so it does not exercise any April or May date.

**Open points (my recommendation for each):**
1. `GER-OBNW-000H` (OB Nordwest) is a placeholder that is not used: OB Nordwest is Army Group H renamed, and all rows stay on `GER-AG-00HG` with the renaming in the notes of `C1201`. Recommendation: keep as it is; merge or remove the placeholder only if Scott wants a separate OB Nordwest unit.
2. 15 of the 18 closures inferred from the next claim rest on a Lexikon April table row: 12 month-level rows (0.3, the 15th as an approximate date) close the earlier relation on 14 April (for example the 7th Fallschirmjäger Division leaves the LXXXVI Corps on 14 April) and 3 rows with a day close it the day before. Recommendation: keep; they are inferred ends, annotated, and the alternative (leave them open until the surrender) is no better sourced.
3. Six units were added: the Twelfth Army (Lexikon page and CMH) and the 2nd Mountain Division (Lexikon page) have a formation date; Army Blumentritt, Panzer Division von Clausewitz, Division Scharnhorst and Division Ulrich von Hutten rest on CMH alone and have no formation date. Recommendation: keep.
4. The 6th SS Mountain Division Nord is activated with one row (`C1274`, 2 April, 0.3, a representative point at Limburg, commander named "General Brenner" as in CMH). Recommendation: keep; it is the only evidence in the sources read.
5. Army Group G: the Lexikon dissolution order of 29 April (`C1199`, 0.3) is not in CMH, which treats Army Group G as the surrendering command on 5 May (`C1200`, 0.9). Recommendation: keep both; the relation stays open.
6. The Fifth Panzer Army ends on 17 April (Lexikon; `C1268`, 0.5) while CMH gives no day for Harpe's capture, only the surrenders of 16 to 18 April. Recommendation: keep one row with the Lexikon date.
7. Surrender signing places (Haar, Innsbruck, Karlsbad, Oldenburg, Lüneburg Heath, Reims, Berlin) are `sector_center` rows whose caveat says they are not headquarters. Recommendation: keep; the schema has no signing-place value.
8. The Twenty-fourth Army comes from CMH alone (the Lexikon page of that number is another army), and its attached relation to the Nineteenth Army raises the seventh validator warning. Recommendation: accept the warning as part of the baseline.
9. XVIII SS Corps (Lexikon) or XVIII SS Panzer Corps (CMH): one unit. The Seventh Army was under OB West for a day or two around 9 and 10 April; only the 28 April row (`C1205`) states the move. Recommendation: keep.
10. Lüneburg Heath: the place (the Timeloberg at Wendisch Evern) rests on English Wikipedia (`R661`); the date, hour and terms rest on the Avalon text and CMH; the row is 0.7. Recommendation: keep.

## German Batch 3: German units, 1 November to 31 December 1944 (27 October 2026)

Branch `german-batch3-nov-dec-1944` (branched from `main` `dcd2aec`, PR #21), merged to `main` as PR #22 (`c1ebd61`) on 28 October 2026. Scope approved by Scott on 27 October 2026 ("Go ahead"): German units at division level and above, 1 November to 31 December 1944, same method, sources, confidence rubric and low-confidence allowance as German Batch 2. Sources: CMH Green Books (Siegfried Line, Lorraine, Riviera to the Rhine, Ardennes; one family), the Lexikon der Wehrmacht tables, the Nafziger Collection (944GLAA, the 15 December 1944 order of battle, a separate family used for the 16 December parents), Wikipedia and OpenStreetMap Nominatim for coordinates only. No Feldgrau, no German Wikipedia as a sole source.

Done: 121 claims, `C0955` to `C1075` (confidence 0.5 for 88 rows, 0.3 for 33, none at 0.7), for 91 units. 37 placeholders became active (Army Group H, the Sixth Panzer Army and the Twenty-fifth Army; LIII, LXIII, LXVII, LXXXV, LXXXIX, XC and XXXIX Panzer Corps; and 27 divisions listed in `docs/changelog.md`) and two units were added, `GER-COR-KVOSG` (Corps Command Vosges) and `GER-COR-FELDT` (Korps Feldt). Command level: OB West (Ziegenberg, 26 November), Army Group H (10 November), the Nineteenth Army under Army Group Oberrhein (Rasp replaces Wiese, 15 December, `C1018`), the Fifth Panzer Army moving to Army Group B (23 October), the Sixth Panzer Army (7 November, 16 December), First, Fifth Panzer, Seventh, Fifteenth, Nineteenth and Twenty-fifth Armies. Corps and divisions: the Aachen, Hürtgen and Roer defence, the 16 December Ardennes order of battle (I SS Panzer, LXVI, LVIII Panzer, XLVII Panzer, LXXXV, LXVII Corps and their divisions), Lorraine and the Saar (LXXXIX, LVIII, XIII SS, LXXXII Corps, 361st, 559th, 48th, 21st Panzer, 36th, 17th SS, 462nd, 11th Panzer, Panzer Lehr, 19th and 347th), the Vosges, Belfort and Colmar (Nineteenth Army, LXIV, IV Luftwaffe Field, XC, LXIII, LXXXV Corps, 159th, 189th, 338th, 553rd, 708th, 716th, 16th, 198th, 256th), the Netherlands and the Scheldt (70th Division at Middelburg on 6 November, Army Group H, First Parachute Army, Twenty-fifth Army, Korps Feldt, LXXXVI and LXXXVIII Corps) and Lexikon month-level rows at confidence 0.3 for 15 November and 15 December.

Earlier relations: 35 were closed as inferred from the next claim (`CR0011` to `CR0671`; listed with old value, new value and reason in `docs/changelog.md`); the Panzer Lehr (`CR0634`) and LVIII Panzer Corps (`CR0602`, `CR0709`, `CR0718`) cases were fixed by hand because later claims already linked them. No existing claim was changed.

**What German Batch 3 does NOT cover:**
- Army Group Oberrhein has no claim of its own (no headquarters place in the sources read; it stays a placeholder unit and appears in the Nineteenth Army row `C1018`), Army Groups G and B have no November or December headquarters row, and the 1945 German coverage after 31 December 1944 is untouched.
- Lexikon rows skipped because they conflict with CMH (11th Panzer, Panzer Lehr, 344th and 347th in December; 353rd on 27 November), are garbled (3rd Fallschirmjäger December "LVII", 49th November "LXXXXII", 10th SS November) or name another theatre or no place (711th "Ungarn", 64th November, 331st, 246th Volksgrenadier, LIII Corps, LXVII Corps December, 59th Division, 2nd and 9th SS, 2nd Fallschirmjäger 6 December).
- The 30th SS Division, Panzer brigades (including the 106th) and Kampfgruppen not already in the dataset; the 319th Division (Channel Islands, out of scope).
- The 361st Volksgrenadier Division's 951st Regiment at Raon-l'Étape (same day as the Moyenvic claim); Group Hoehne appears in the context of `C1035` only.
- Operations: no Batch 3 row has a blank `operation_id`. The Hürtgen and Roer rows carried broader operations (`OP-SIEGFRIED-LINE`, `OP-QUEEN`, `OP-CAMP-RHINELAND`) when Batch 3 was merged; the follow-up branch `huertgen-roer-operations` added `OP-HUERTGEN` and `OP-ROER-PLAIN` and linked nine rows to them (the others stay as they were; see `docs/changelog.md`). The Haguenau and Strasbourg rows use `OP-VOSGES`. An earlier version of this line wrongly said the Hürtgen and Roer rows were blank.

Open points for Scott: (1) The Lexikon (2 December) and CMH (10 December) give different creation dates for Army Group Oberrhein; the difference is stated in the note of `C1018`, which rests on 15 December (Rasp takes over, both families agree). (2) Army Group H on 10 November (Lexikon) against 11 November (CMH): both kept. (3) Group Hoehne is read as the LXXXIX Corps (`C1035`). (4) Utrecht and Tilburg are representative points with a stated precision, not exact headquarters. (5) The two new units `GER-COR-KVOSG` and `GER-COR-FELDT`. (6) The Lexikon and CMH "15. Armee" rows that belong to the Netherlands deception headquarters are mapped to the Twenty-fifth Army (`GER-ARMY-025A`) with notes; the real Fifteenth Army under Zangen stays under Army Group B. (7) The 462nd and 708th divisions use Volksgrenadier ids (`GER-VG-462D`, `GER-VG-708D`) while the 347th, 198th and 269th keep `INF` ids.

## XXII and XXIII Corps (26 October 2026)
