Branch `corps-xxii-xxiii`, based on `batch4-jan-mar-1945` (neither merged; no pull request). Task approved by Scott on 26 October 2026: activate the two corps with sourced claims and command relations so that the divisions assigned to them have parents; claims through 31 March 1945 only.

Done: 5 claims, `C0642` to `C0646` (XXII Corps: the advance party at Doddington Hall on 10 November 1944, the command post at Grevenbroich on 28 March 1945, the corps becoming operational on 30 March, and the divisions the CMH tables list under it on 31 March; XXIII Corps: the command post at Vouziers on 28 March); both corps are active; 7 relations `CR0472` to `CR0478` (both corps to the Fifteenth Army from 21 January 1945; the 97th, 94th, 86th, 95th and 82nd Airborne Divisions to XXII Corps from 28 to 31 March); the open relation `CR0097` (82nd Airborne Division to V Corps) was closed because it overlapped the new one. Sources: the History of the Fifteenth US Army (Letters of Instructions Thirteen and Fourteen of 28 March 1945), CMH ETO-OB tables and the Combat Chronicle of the 82nd Airborne Division, Wikipedia, TheSecondWar.pl, The Supreme Command appendix D.

Not covered (left for Batch 5, 1 April to 8 May 1945; sources already read are noted):
- XXII Corps: the relief of the divisions in the line on 4 April by the 94th Infantry, 101st Airborne and 82nd Airborne Divisions, the extension of the sector to Homberg by 5 April, the control of Regierungsbezirk Aachen by 15 April, the command post moves (Grevenbroich to Hilden on 24 April, opening 25 April), the 17th Airborne Division from 27 April, the 28th Infantry Division from 13 to 25 April, and the occupation to 15 June (History of the Fifteenth US Army pp. 46 to 56, 90 to 100).
- XXIII Corps: the command post at Oberstein (Idar-Oberstein) from 10 April, the occupation of the Twelfth Army Group zone by 20 April and of the remainder of the Saarland, Hessen west of the Rhine and the Pfalz by 27 April, the 28th Infantry Division from 26 April, and the handover to the French on 10 July (same History; Wikipedia).
- Relation ends from the CMH tables that lie in April (read, not recorded because the rows are outside the batch window): the 97th Infantry Division leaves XXII Corps on the row of 1 April (last day 31 March), the 95th Infantry Division's operational control ends with the row of 2 April (last day 1 April), the 86th Infantry Division's with the row of 5 April (last day 4 April), the 82nd Airborne Division's with the row of 30 April (last day 29 April); the 94th Infantry Division stays; the 101st Airborne Division (1 April), 20th Armored Division (3 April), 17th Airborne Division (27 April) and 28th Infantry Division (13 April, then XXIII Corps on 26 April) enter the corps column later.
- Division-side relations and claims: the army-column relations of these divisions (Fifteenth Army from 28 to 31 March for the 94th, 95th, 97th and 86th) and claims of the 86th, 94th, 95th, 97th Infantry and 82nd Airborne Divisions from 28 March are not made (94th: last claim 22 February; 95th: 5 March; 86th and 97th: no claim yet). `CR0447` (95th Division to XIX Corps, attached, open from 1 March) is still open although the CMH table has the division under XXII Corps from 31 March; left for the 95th Division's own claims.
- Open points for Scott: (1) the `formed_date` of XXII and XXIII Corps: **done on the branch `corps-followups`** (both 1944-01-15, the activation date; see the changelog entry); (2) the start of both corps' assignment to the Fifteenth Army (21 January 1945) rests on two secondary accounts: **accepted by Scott on 26 October 2026**; the notes of CR0472 and CR0473 already say so, no data change; (3) the 102nd Infantry Division is not related to XXII Corps (the History and the CMH table disagree); (4) the 82nd Airborne Division relation is `attached` on the Combat Chronicle's wording although the ETO-OB corps column does not say so (accepted by Scott on 26 October 2026); (5) the dates of Harmon's takeover of XXII Corps differ (14 to 23 January 1945); (6) **CR0447** (95th Infantry Division to XIX Corps) was closed on 30 March 1945 on the branch `corps-followups`, a table-based end; the 95th Division's table and Combat Chronicle list XXII Corps (Fifteenth Army) only on 31 March and 1 April and XIX Corps (Ninth Army) again from 2 April, so **CR0477** (95th to XXII Corps, open) needs the end 1 April 1945 and the 95th Division needs its XIX Corps and Ninth Army relations from 2 April: Batch 5 (not done here because the pass stops at 31 March).

## Ordered / effective / exercised states (proposed 25 October 2026, cheap form implemented)

A command change has up to four dates: intended (a plan or tentative selection), ordered, effective and exercised. Implemented cheaply: `ordered_date`, `effective_from` with optional `effective_time`, inclusive `effective_to` with `effective_to_time`, narrative rows as the exercised evidence, `other` rows for a decision that is not yet an order. Example motivating a state column: the 7th Armored Division's move to St. Vith. The 12th Army Group staff chose the division on the afternoon of 16 December (intended), General Hasbrouck was alerted by telephone at 1730 (ordered), the advance party went to VIII Corps at Bastogne for instructions, where Middleton outlined the mission and left the commitment of each combat command to Hasbrouck (effective and exercised on arrival; Ardennes p273-274). Roadmap, not done: a `command_state` value per relationship entry (intended / ordered / effective / exercised) and a derived view that answers "who could give this unit orders at this hour", which needs hour-level data the Green Books rarely give.

- **Panzer-Brigades 105, 106, 107, 108, 111, 112 and 113 (24 claims, C1759 to C1782).** The seven placeholders are now active, with formation dates (Wikipedia, List of German brigades).
- **Panzer-Brigade 150, the Führer-Begleit and Führer-Grenadier Brigades, SS-Panzer-Brigade Westfalen (11 claims, C1783 to C1793).** `GER-PZB-150B` is active. `GER-PG-FUEBE` and `GER-PG-FUEGR` are active, with their echelon changed from division to brigade and their names showing the division from 26 January 1945. `GER-SSPZB-WESTF` is new.
- **Kampfgruppen Chill, Peiper and von der Heydte (10 claims, C1794 to C1803).** These are new units. `GER-KG-PEIPER` is organic to the 1st SS Panzer Division (`CR1062`), and `GER-KG-CHILL` is organic to the 85th Infantry Division (`CR1061`). `GER-KG-HEYDTE` (Luftwaffe) has no relation.
- **Fortress garrisons (8 claims, C1804 to C1811).** The garrisons are their own category, with echelon `other`: `GER-FEST-LORIENT` (under the XXV Corps, `CR1063`, until 10 May 1945), `GER-FEST-STNAZ`, `GER-FEST-DUNKIRK` and `GER-FEST-LAROCH` (no relation).
- **Inferred values** are annotated in the rows: representative points (Albert Canal, Meuse-Escaut Canal), approximate dates (Woensdrecht 10 October, Dunkirk 9 September, La Rochelle 1 September, Führer-Grenadier reserve 31 December), relation starts at row dates, and relation ends taken from dissolution dates.
- **Panzer-Brigade 103 has no claims.** It served on the Eastern Front only.
- **Validator:** one new warning, `GER-PZB-108B` line 3 (an attachment to the 246th Volks Grenadier Division with no standing parent stated). It is explained in the row's Relationship note.
- **Semantic tests:** scenario 25 is new. Organic relations are kept out of `build/command_relations.csv` by design, so the Peiper and Chill checks read the master table.

Resolved: Scott accepted all ten recommendations on 29 October 2026 (PR #38), with no data change. **Panzer-Brigade 103 is dropped from the brigade list**: it served on the Eastern Front only, so the approved list now has 34 brigades. The open points were removed in Clean-up 3.

6. **Not written:** strengths (tank and battalion counts in all four manuscripts) stay in the reading notes; the dated front lines of the B-475 annex map and the B-763 and B-179 sketches were not traced; Allied dates in the manuscripts were not used.


## Future task: exact translations of public-domain FMS manuscripts

Make exact, complete English translations of the scanned German FMS manuscripts used here (B-475, B-235, B-179, B-763 and later ones), including their maps, sketches and figures, working only from free public copies (the Combined Arms Research Library digital collection or NARA RG 338 scans). Do not use Fold3 images for this. Each translation should keep the page and image numbers of the scan so claim locators can cite it, and mark illegible words rather than guess them.

## FMS batch five (B-781, B-831, B-084, B-849; branch `fms-german-5`, 31 October 2026): open points

1. **Parents left blank** on all 39 new rows (`C2209` to `C2247`) under the rule of no command relation from a single manuscript. Recommendation: fill them only where an existing relation already covers the date, in a later clean-up with the observed_during links.
2. **LXIII Corps start in November 1944.** B-781 has the new LXIII Corps under Schack formed just before the French attack of 14 November when the LXXXV Corps staff left Belfort; `C1023` has the Dehner organization becoming the LXIII Corps on 18 November. Recommendation: keep `C1023`; check de Lattre and CMH Riviera to the Rhine before any change.
3. **Wiese's handover.** B-781 has 18 December 1944, as does `C2113` (Botsch); `C1018` has 15 December. Recommendation: leave both; open a conflict group only if the CMH wording turns out to be a firm date rather than a by-date.
4. **8th Parachute Division in late February 1945.** B-084 has it moved from the Roermond area to the north wing from 16 February (new row `C2235`); `C1155` has it fighting west of Mönchengladbach on 27 February. Recommendation: keep `C1155` (CMH); it is probably a detached Kampfgruppe; check CMH Last Offensive and Stacey.
5. **2nd Parachute Division in February 1945.** B-084 has it brought from Holland about 27 February; `C1129` lists it under the LXXXVI Corps on the lower Rhine on 8 February. Recommendation: keep `C1129`; part of the division was likely already there; no change without a second source.
6. **End of the Fifteenth Army in the Ruhr pocket.** B-849 has organised fighting ended effective 15 April with a last radio message that day; `C1215` has the army surrendering on 13 April. Recommendation: keep `C1215`; add the 15 April end only with a second source.
7. **LXXXI Corps commander on 11-13 April 1945.** B-849 reads as the commanding general captured on the evening of 11 April with Burdach in charge from 12 April, but the line is faint; `C1230` has Köchling surrendering on 13 April. Recommendation: keep `C1230`; treat the B-849 reading as doubtful.
8. **B-831 primary text.** The German scan is illegible, so the English translation was used and some annex digits are doubtful (`C2222` Salm at 0.3). Recommendation: recheck the annex command-post dates against a CARL or NARA copy when the translation task runs.
9. **Not written:** strengths stay in the reading notes; the sketch front lines were not traced; Allied dates were not used; XIII Corps and LXV Corps rows were not added because both are placeholders without claims. Recommendation: add them only in a placeholder-promotion pass with a second source.

## FMS batch six (B-745, B-151; branch `fms-german-6`, 31 October 2026): open points

1. **Parents left blank** on all 34 new rows (`C2248` to `C2281`) under the rule of no command relation from a single manuscript. Recommendation: fill them only where an existing relation already covers the date, in a later clean-up with the observed_during links.
2. **Brandenberger's takeover of the Nineteenth Army.** B-745 has him arriving at Dettenbach on 28 March 1945 and taking command from 1 April; `C1190` (Lexikon) has 26 March. Recommendation: keep `C1190`; open a conflict group only if a second source gives 1 April.
3. **LXXX Corps placed under the Nineteenth Army.** B-745 has 9 April (Anlage 1a and the text); `C2123` has the Army Group G order of 8 April. Recommendation: no change; read as the order on 8 April taking effect on 9 April.
4. **End of the XVIII SS Corps.** B-745 has the corps staff dissolved at 2200 on 26 April 1945 near Immendingen; `C1273` has the corps ceasing to exist on 27 April. Recommendation: keep `C1273`; one day apart and both sources are thin on this corps.
5. **Twenty-fourth Army across Lake Constance.** B-745 has the army shipping its troops to Meersburg and Bregenz in the night to 26 April with its command post at Eglofs (new row `C2268`); `C1219` has the crossing on 21 April. Recommendation: keep `C1219`; check CMH Riviera to the Rhine and de Lattre for the date of the boat move before any change.
6. **Placeholders not given a first claim.** B-745 gives dated command posts for the 106th Infantry Division (Malsch), Division Nr. 405 (Bottenau), Division Nr. 905 (Bahlingen; the text types 805) and the 257th Volksgrenadier Division (Ettlingen line), but all four are placeholders without claims. Recommendation: add them in a placeholder-promotion pass with a second source.
7. **Fifth Panzer Army leaving the Aachen sector.** B-151 has the operations staff moving to Manderscheid on 23 November 1944 (new row `C2277`); `C0956` has the headquarters disengaged east of the Roer on 15 November and `C2179` (B-235) has the move to Manderscheid about 30 November. Recommendation: keep both; B-151 is the commander's own date and B-235 gives no day, so add a conflict group only if a third source dates the move.
8. **Which Manderscheid.** `PL1066` (used by `C2179`) has the coordinates of Manderscheid near Arzfeld in the western Eifel; B-151 puts the army staff close to Eisenschmitt and Gerolstein, which fits Manderscheid in the Bernkastel-Wittlich district (new place `PL1139`). Recommendation: move `C2179` to `PL1139` in a place clean-up, with Scott's approval, after checking B-235 for any hint of the western Manderscheid.
9. **Not written:** strengths, tank counts and refit percentages stay in the reading notes; the B-745 sketch front lines were not traced; Allied dates were not used. Recommendation: no action.

## Future task: obtain FMS B-151a (Manteuffel, Fifth Panzer Army in the Ardennes battle)

B-151a, the sequel to B-151 that covers the course of the Ardennes offensive from 16 December 1944 to 25 January 1945 (part IV of Manteuffel's outline), is not online: the Internet Archive item catalogued as B-151a is B-151 itself, and neither manuscript is on the NARA M1035 fiche used by Fold3. Obtain a copy from the Combined Arms Research Library (CARL) digital collection or from NARA RG 338, then read it under the same rules (German original first if one can be had, English translation otherwise).

## 12th Army Group situation maps, pass 1 (branch `loc-12ag-maps`, 31 October 2026): open points

Batch 1 read the eight weekly Normandy sheets of 6 June to 25 July 1944 (`C2282` to `C2468`, reference `R781`). 73 new rows have an existing row of the same unit within three days and 42 of them lie within 10 km (58%); 123 new rows fall between documented dates with no existing row within two days. Resume with the sheet of 1 August 1944 (`cs000057`); the sheets from August on use other base sheets, so each new base sheet needs its own graticule control file in `tools/loc12ag/`.

1. **Parents blank and commander unknown** on all 187 new rows. Recommendation: fill parents only where an existing relation already covers the date, in a later clean-up; leave the commander unknown unless a row of the same unit and date names one.
2. **Main body against objective or front-line points.** Most of the 31 new rows more than 10 km from an existing row within three days compare the map's main-body point with an objective, front-line point or sector centre 10-20 km away. Recommendation: no change; the rows describe different things.
3. **Goodwood, 18 July 1944.** The map of 1200 hrs 18 July has the Guards Armoured Division near Tracy-sur-Mer (`C2425`) and the 7th Armoured Division near Carcagny (`C2427`), 35 km and 28 km from the Goodwood rows `C1419` and `C1422`; the 11th Armoured Division near Cagny agrees (`C2436`). Recommendation: keep `C1419` and `C1422`; the map probably lags the overnight move. Read the sheet of 19 July before any change.
4. **Panzer Lehr Division, 18 July 1944.** The map has it near La Couture west of Saint-Lô (`C2413`), 35 km from `C0791` and `C0792` dated 15 July. Recommendation: keep both; the division had moved west by mid-July, so check the dates of `C0791` and `C0792` against Ellis and Harrison.
5. **I SS Panzer Corps headquarters** on 27 June and 4 July (`C2339`, `C2365`) is 23-26 km south of the sector-centre rows `C0160` and `C0769`. Recommendation: no change; a headquarters behind the front against a sector centre.
6. **Other differences over 20 km:** 91st Luftlande Division on 6 June (`C2283` against `C0010`), 2nd SS Panzer Division on 11 July (`C2379` against `C0796`), 12th SS Panzer Division on 11 July (`C2391` against `C0778`), 59th Infantry Division on 11 July (`C2397` against `C1410`) and 271st Infantry Division on 18 July (`C2418` against `C0811`). Recommendation: keep the existing rows; check each with a second source in a later pass.
7. **Placeholders seen on the maps:** the 265th, 266th and 276th Infantry Divisions and the 16th Luftwaffe Field Division appear on several sheets but have no claims. Recommendation: promote them in a placeholder pass with a second source, then add the map readings from the private notes.
8. **Three readings not written** for want of a gazetteer place within 3 km (353rd Infantry Division on 20 June, 2nd SS Panzer Division on 4 July, 77th Infantry Division on 18 July). Recommendation: add hamlet-level places for them in the next batch.
9. **Modern commune names:** `PL1154` (Val de Drôme) and `PL1156` (Moulins en Bessin) are post-2016 merged communes. Recommendation: rename them to the 1944 communes in a place clean-up.
10. **US units not read in batch 1.** Normandy US coverage is dense. Recommendation: apply the rule of US units only where the dataset has no row within six days from the August sheets on.
11. **Not written:** brigades, regiments, units marked elements, unlocated, doubtful or en route, and front lines. Recommendation: no action.
12. **Batch 2us (US units, Normandy, `C2469` to `C2582`): corps and army headquarters against objective rows.** All eight checkable US headquarters rows are 14-54 km from existing objective or sector-centre rows of the same corps or army (for example First Army on 27 June, `C2511` against `C0181`). Recommendation: no change; a command post is not an objective.
13. **US divisions over 10 km:** 2nd Armored 13 June (`C2476`, 30 km from `C0100`) and 18 July (`C2556`, 16 km from `C0224`), 9th 20 June (`C2479`, 22 km from `C0135`), 79th 20 June (`C2480`, 17 km from `C0140`) and 25 July (`C2564`, 31 km from `C0446`), 90th 20 June (`C2482`, 22 km from `C0142`), 8th 4 July (`C2513`, 19 km from `C0375`). One Nominatim request was dropped ("remote end closed connection") and the town search was re-run after a 60-second pause with the cache. 103. **`PL2002` Rott mismatch: cause found (read-only, row not changed).** The `PL2002` row (50.6834, 6.2116) is correct for Rott. The snap step made a candidate from the query "Roetgen" at 50.6472, 6.1968; reverse geocoding returned village "Rott" (Rott is a district of the Roetgen municipality), so the candidate was renamed "Rott" and merged by name into `PL2002`, about 4 km away. The 78th Division reading (about 50.628, 6.220) is 2.7 km from the candidate but 6.2 km from `PL2002`, so the auto-fix moved it to Petergensfeld (`C5455` this week, as `C4880`–`C5337`). Cause: naming and merge step, not the map, registration or another Rott (GeoNames has a second Rott at 50.665, 6.115). Recommendation (Scott decides): add Roetgen as its own place, and make the name merge refuse joins over 3 km apart.
104. **Ulm near Renchen.** Reverse geocoding gave "Ulm, Arrondissement de l'Ortenau", which matched `PL0851` Ulm (Danube) 144 km away; added as a new place "Ulm (Renchen), Baden-Württemberg" for `C5534` (XIV SS Corps). Recommendation: check the XIV SS Corps reading on the next sheet.

(b) Name rules for new gazetteer candidates (`tools/loc12ag/name_merge.py`): a candidate within 1.5 km of a GeoNames municipality seat takes the municipality name, and a candidate whose name equals an existing place more than 3 km away is not merged into it but becomes a new place named "<name> (<nearest municipality>)". Tested on the stored candidates of batches 5m-5r: only the Rott candidate is refused each week. The 78th Division rows `C4880`, `C4988`, `C5103`, `C5216`, `C5337`, `C5455`, `C5588` and `C5717` were moved from `PL2230` Petergensfeld (3.7 km from the reading) to `PL2611` Roetgen (2.8-2.9 km), with old/new/reason notes. `PL2002` (50.6834, 6.2116) unchanged and correct for Rott. 136. **Sheet of 20 March 1945 (`cs000289`): all tiles read, plus an extra Palatinate tile.** Third Army had moved east of the six standard tiles, so a seventh tile (x 4600-6200, y 2300-4100) was read; positions on this sheet were taken from pixel positions on the tiles, not from the printed grid labels. Moved: Third Army VIII, XII and XX Corps, 4th, 10th, 11th and 12th Armored, 5th, 26th, 65th, 76th, 80th, 87th, 89th, 90th and 94th Divisions into the Palatinate and toward Mainz; Seventh Army XV and XXI Corps, 6th Armored, 36th, 42nd, 44th, 45th, 71st, 100th and 103rd Divisions to the Westwall; Ninth and First Army units on the Rhine; British Second Army and VIII Corps behind the Wesel front. Not written: German boxes in the Wiesbaden "unlocated" panel, the two "256" boxes (not tied to one row), the Remagen bridgehead boxes, Divisions z.b.V. and "?" boxes. Dropped (label with no box on this sheet): 28 rows (16 German divisions that crossed the Rhine, and US 2nd, 4th, 9th, 28th, 78th, 99th, 106th Divisions, 5th and 14th Armored, 17th and 82nd Airborne, XVIII Airborne Corps). Registration median residual 3.18 px is higher than usual. The 1:1 and 3:3 differences of 33-54 km for the 5th and 12th Armored rows are larger than map error. Recommendation: check the Third Army rows against Patton's after action report, and look for the dropped First Army divisions in the Remagen bridgehead on the next sheet.

218. **47th Volksgrenadier Division, Ahrem in late January 1945 (open, 2 November 2026).** The 12th Army Group map rows `C5237` and `C5357` (16 and 23 January 1945) place the division near Ahrem west of Cologne; the division's own files on NARA T-315 roll 940 (`R783`) show it taking over the 245th Infantry Division sector in the Haguenau Forest by 3 February (`C7063`) and receiving XC Corps papers of 29 January. The map rows are unchanged (different dates, no conflict group); the move date from the Rur to Alsace (late January) needs a second source. NARA T-311 Army Group B rolls or T-312 AOK 1/AOK 15 rolls may settle it.
219. **64th and 70th Infantry Divisions, early October 1944 (open, 2 November 2026).** The 12th Army Group map rows `C3405` (64th, near Leerdam) and `C3193` (70th, near Hellouw), both 3 October 1944, place the divisions north of the Waal; the Army Group B files on NARA T-311 roll 4 (`R784`) have the 64th in Festung Schelde Süd (the Breskens pocket, `C7090`, 5 October) and the 70th on Walcheren (`C7091`, 6 October). The map rows are unchanged (different dates, no conflict group); the map labels may be misread unit numbers and should be checked on the sheets.
220. **LXXXI Corps command post, September-October 1944 (open, 2 November 2026).** T-311 roll 4 gives LXXXI Corps at Niederzier southeast of Jülich on 22 September (`C7085`) and 10 October (`C7094`) and at Güsten northeast of Jülich on 4 October (`C7087`); an order of 3 October has guides for the OB West visit to the corps post wait at the southeast edge of Düren. Either the post moved twice or one entry names a rear or forward echelon; a corps file (T-314) would settle it.
221. **190th Infantry Division before its formation date (open, 2 November 2026).** `units.csv` gives the 190th Infantry Division a formation date of 4 November 1944 (Lexikon der Wehrmacht), but the Army Group B report of 5 October 1944 on T-311 roll 4 already names the Wehrkreis X Walküre division (Kampfgruppe Knaust) south of Nijmegen as the 190th Infantry Division (`C7089`). Either the formation date is the official renaming of Division Nr. 190 or the October row belongs to a predecessor record; the record was promoted from placeholder to active for this row.

### Cole, The Ardennes (Fifth Panzer Army before Bastogne and the Meuse (chs. 13, 19, 22, 24); 3 November 2026)

226. **Panzer Lehr Division, 19 and 23-24 December 1944 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** Cole (`R384`, ch. 13, p. 297; `C7146`) has a Panzer Lehr kampfgruppe in Mageret before midnight on 18 December and the division at Wardin and before Bastogne on 19 December, while the 12th Army Group map row `C4752` puts it at Wahlhausen on 19 December, about 15 km north-east. Cole (`R388`, ch. 18, pp. 437-440) also has the main attack on Rochefort start after midnight on 23-24 December and the town taken on 24 December, while `C2185` (FMS B-235) dates the capture to 23 December. Existing rows kept. Recommendation: lower `C4752` to 0.3 as a lagging map position; keep `C2185` and add an evening-23/24 December note on Scott's decision. **Resolved (branch `confidence-corroborated`, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** conflict group G035 (C7146 preferred; C4752 alternate, already 0.3); C2185 kept with a note on the 23/24 December date; nothing deleted.

227. **2d and 116th Panzer Divisions, 19 December 1944 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** Cole (`R389`, ch. 19, pp. 449-452; `C7148`) has the 2d Panzer Division attacking Noville on 19 December, and (ch. 13, p. 317) the 116th Panzer Division at Bertogne and Salle that day, while the 12th Army Group map rows `C4790` (Leidenborn) and `C4710` (Atzerath) put them 35-40 km east, near the Our. Existing rows kept. Recommendation: lower both map rows to 0.3 or retire them as lagging positions on Scott's decision. **Resolved (branch `confidence-corroborated`, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** conflict group G036 (C7148 preferred; C4790 alternate) and G037 (C7168 preferred; C4710 alternate), both map rows already 0.3; nothing deleted.

228. **3d Panzer Grenadier Division, 19 December 1944 and 2 January 1945 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** Cole has the division at Krinkelt-Rocherath on 19 December (`R804`, ch. 6, p. 121; `C7163`) and in the Bois de Fragotte near Chenogne from 30 December to 1 January (`R709`, ch. 24, pp. 616-621; `C7153`), while the 12th Army Group map rows `C4768` (Thorr, 19 December) and `C4993` (Schöneseiffen, 2 January) put it far to the north-east. Existing rows kept. Recommendation: retire `C4993` (the division had been moved to Bastogne by Hitler's order, Cole p. 580) and lower `C4768` to 0.3, on Scott's decision. **Resolved (branch `confidence-corroborated`, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** conflict group G038 (C7163 preferred; C4768 alternate) and G039 (C7153 preferred; C4993 alternate, kept, not retired); nothing deleted.

### Cole, The Ardennes, second pass (US and British; 3 November 2026)

233. **101st Airborne Division, 19 December 1944 (open, 3 November 2026).** Cole (`R384`, ch. 13, p. 307) has McAuliffe select an assembly area around Mande-Saint-Étienne, and the 12th Army Group map row `C4787` agrees; the text row `C0553` places the arrival at Bastogne itself, about 6 km east. Existing row kept. Recommendation: keep `C0553` (the division did arrive at Bastogne) and add a location qualifier naming the Mande-Saint-Étienne assembly area, on Scott's decision.

234. **28th Infantry Division, 19 December 1944 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** Cole (`R393`, ch. 8, p. 208; `C7188`) moves the division headquarters from Wiltz to Sibret on the morning of 19 December; the 12th Army Group map row `C4715` puts the division near Liefrange, about 17 km east. Existing row kept. Recommendation: the map symbol probably marks the remnants of the 109th or 110th Infantry, not the division; lower `C4715` to 0.3. **Resolved (branch `confidence-corroborated`, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** conflict group G048 (C7188 preferred; C4715 alternate, confidence 0.5 to 0.3); nothing deleted.

235. **11th Armored Division, 23-24 December 1944 (open, 3 November 2026).** `C1921` has the division defending the Meuse from Givet on 23 December; Cole (`R708`, ch. 22, p. 560; `C7215`) says the sector was given to VIII Corps on 23 December and part of the division reached the west bank on the following day. Existing row kept. Recommendation: re-date `C1921` to 24 December or mark it as the date of assignment, on Scott's decision.

236. **British 30 Corps, 19 December 1944 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** Cole (`R708`, ch. 22, p. 557; `C7211`) has Horrocks ordered at 1730 on 19 December to move 30 Corps from Boxtel to the Liège-Brussels area; the 12th Army Group map row `C4688` shows the corps near Schootshei (Rotem), about 64 km south-east. Existing row kept. Recommendation: lower `C4688` to 0.3 (the symbol may show the corps' leading elements or a lagging position). **Resolved (branch `confidence-corroborated`, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** conflict group G049 (C7211 preferred; C4688 alternate, confidence 0.5 to 0.3); nothing deleted.

237. **43rd (Wessex) Infantry Division, 4 July 1944 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** Stacey (`R668`, p. 155; `C7238`) has troops of the division in Verson and Fontaine-Étoupefour on 4 July, withdrawn that night; the 12th Army Group map row `C2368` puts the division near Carcagny, about 13 km north-west. Existing row kept. Recommendation: conflict group with `C7238` preferred and `C2368` alternate (the map symbol is probably a lagging or rear position), on Scott's decision. **Resolved (branch `stacey-victory-campaign`, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** conflict group G051 (C7238 preferred; C2368 alternate, confidence unchanged at 0.5); nothing deleted.

238. **12th SS Panzer Division, 11 July 1944 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** Stacey (`R668`, p. 164) says the division handed the Caen sector to the 1st SS Panzer Division on 11 July and was withdrawn to refit; `C0778` (Lexikon der Wehrmacht and CMH) has it relieved by the 272nd Infantry Division and sent to refit near Potigny. Both agree on the withdrawal; they differ on the relieving formation (the 1st SS took over the sector, the 272nd may have relieved part of the line). Existing row kept, Stacey not added to it. Recommendation: add a note to `C0778` naming both relieving formations; no conflict group. **Resolved (branch `stacey-victory-campaign`, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** note added to C0778 naming both relieving formations (the 1st SS Panzer Division per Stacey, the 272nd Infantry Division per the Lexikon); no conflict group, no confidence or reference change.

239. **II SS Panzer Corps headquarters, 5 September 1944 (conflict group `G050`, 3 November 2026).** Stacey (`R668`, p. 313 n.; `C7288`) says H.Q. 2nd SS Panzer Corps was ordered to Eindhoven on 5 September to supervise the rehabilitation of armoured remnants; `C0853` (`R608`) has Model ordering it to the vicinity of Arnhem the same day. Existing text row kept as preferred, the Stacey row kept as alternate at 0.3. Recommendation: keep `C0853` preferred (the corps was in fact at Arnhem by 17 September); check the Army Group B war diary for the 5 September order if a German source is read later.

240. **Battle Group Chill, 4-6 October 1944 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** `C2174` (`R771`, 0.3) has Kampfgruppe Chill arriving south of Tilburg on 4 October and throwing the Poles back; Stacey (`R668`, p. 382) says the counter-attack that checked the Poles on 4 October was by local reserves and that the 85th Division (Battle Group Chill) was heavily committed covering Tilburg only on 6 October (new row `C7348`). The rows have different dates, so no conflict group. Recommendation: keep both; if a German source (Army Group B or Fifteenth Army war diary) is read later, date Chill's arrival and lower whichever row it contradicts. Still open (Stacey follow-up, 3 November 2026): recommendation is to keep both until a German source settles it; data unchanged. Updated (FMS B-717 reading, branch `fms-fold3-netherlands`, 3 November 2026): Student (`R807`, English p.6) covers Chill only in early September, when the 85th Division commander reported at Moergestel and was given the Beringen sector (new row `C7724`, 6 September, 0.3); he says nothing of Tilburg in October, and Meindl (`R806`) does not mention Chill. Recommendation (updated): still keep both `C2174` and `C7348` ungrouped; the FMS manuscripts read so far cannot date Chill's arrival at Tilburg, so the deciding source remains the Fifteenth Army or LXVII Corps war diary (NARA T-312 / T-314); if none is read, lower `C2174` to the 6 October date of the Stacey row only after Scott approves. Updated (FMS B-774 reading, 3 November 2026): Prittwitz (`R809`, German p.3; new row `C7799`, 4 October, 0.5) has parts of the 85th Division, pulled out at Schijndel and put in south of Tilburg, attack the penetration north of Poppel on 4 October and gain ground until 7 October; B-774 is appended to `C2174` (same family as B-475, confidence held at 0.3). Recommendation (updated): close this point by keeping both `C2174`/`C7799` (4 October, first parts at Poppel) and `C7348` (6 October, the division heavily committed covering Tilburg) as consecutive phases, not a conflict; left open until Scott approves the closure. **Closed (Scott approved 3 November 2026, message t293u):** `C2174`/`C7799` (4 October, the first parts of the 85th Division attack at Poppel) and `C7348` (6 October, the division heavily committed covering Tilburg) are kept ungrouped as consecutive phases, not a conflict; no data changed, no confidence changed.

241. **64th Infantry Division, 9-10 October 1944 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** The 12th Army Group map row `C3420` (`R781`, 0.3) puts the division at Overslingeland (Zuid-Holland) on 10 October; Stacey (`R668`, pp. 362-8, 396-8; new row `C7375`) has it holding the Breskens Pocket throughout and committing its reserve against the Braakman landing near Biervliet on 9 October (`check_coordinates.py` speed warning, 106 km in one day). Text row kept; the map row is not changed. Recommendation: put `C3420` and `C7375` in one conflict group with the text row preferred, or lower `C3420` to 0.1 as a likely misread label, after Scott decides on the map-conflict handling. **Resolved (branch `stacey-victory-campaign`, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** conflict group G052 (C7375 preferred; C3420 alternate, already 0.3; not lowered to 0.1, the group is preferred over lowering); nothing deleted.

242. **6th Army Group headquarters, 15 September 1944 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** Stacey's Sketch 23 (`R668`) puts the 6th Army Group symbol near Dijon on 15 September; text row `C0222` has it at Lyon. Not added as a row (a small-scale sketch symbol). Recommendation: keep `C0222`; if a dated source places the headquarters at Dijon or Vittel later in September, add that as a new row. **Resolved (branch `stacey-victory-campaign`, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** no data change: C0222 (Lyon) kept as the only row; the Sketch 23 symbol is not added. Reopen only if a dated source places the headquarters at Dijon or Vittel.

243. **Headquarters First Canadian Army, December 1944 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** Stacey (p. 447; new row `C7436`) says the Germans knew the headquarters was in Tilburg in late December; `C1285` (23 December, 0.3) records Crerar's warning directive at Nijmegen. Recommendation: keep both; on review consider moving `C1285` to Tilburg or marking it a non-headquarters event, since the text names no Nijmegen headquarters. **Resolved (branch `stacey-victory-campaign`, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** conflict group G053 (C7436 Tilburg preferred; C1285 Nijmegen alternate, 0.3); nothing deleted.

244. **15th (Scottish) Division, 13 February 1945 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** The 12th Army Group map row `C5680` (`R781`, 0.5) puts the division at Afferden (Limburg) on 13 February; Stacey (`R668`, p. 483; new row `C7479`) has its 46th Brigade at Hasselt on the Cleve-Calcar road that day, with the division in the Cleve sector since 8 February (`C1490`, `C1492`, 0.7). About 25 km apart. Text rows kept; the map row is not changed. Recommendation: put `C5680` and `C7479` in one conflict group with the text row preferred, or lower `C5680` to 0.3; the map symbol is probably the 52nd Division's sector mislabelled or lagging. **Resolved (branch `stacey-victory-campaign`, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** conflict group G054 (C7479 preferred; C5680 alternate, confidence unchanged at 0.5; the group is used instead of lowering); nothing deleted.

245. **52nd (Lowland) Division, 18-21 February 1945 (open, 3 November 2026).** Stacey (p. 491; new row `C7501`) says the division finished clearing the Afferden area on 18 February; Ellis (`C1495`, `R671`, 0.5) dates the Afferden fighting to 21 February; the map row `C5870` (`R781`, 0.3) puts the division at Waldenrath, about 70 km south, on 20 February. Recommendation: keep `C1495` and `C7501` (different dates, same place); lower or group `C5870` with `C7501` preferred, as the 52nd was then on the Maas under the 30th Corps. **Partly resolved (Stacey follow-up, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** conflict group G055 (C7501 preferred; C5870 Waldenrath map row alternate, already 0.3). Still open: C1495 (Ellis, 21 February) and C7501 (Stacey, 18 February) are kept as a date pair, data unchanged, until a source dates the Afferden fighting.

246. **2nd Parachute Corps, 19-20 February 1945 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** Stacey (p. 491; new row `C7499`, 0.3 sector row) has H.Q. 2nd Parachute Corps (Meindl) taking over the front on both sides of Goch early on 19 February; the map row `C5809` (`R781`, 0.3) puts the corps at Varsselder (Gelderland), about 40 km north-east, on 20 February. Recommendation: keep both at 0.3 for now; the Schlemm or Meindl manuscripts (`R777`) should settle the headquarters place, after which group them with the dated row preferred. Still open (Stacey follow-up, 3 November 2026): recommendation is to keep both until the Schlemm or Meindl manuscripts settle it; data unchanged. **Resolved (branch `fms-fold3-netherlands`, FMS B-093 reading, 3 November 2026):** Meindl (`R806`, German p.12, Sketch 3) arrived with the corps staff at Sonsbeck on the forenoon of 19 February and commanded the Goch sector from there until 3 March. New row `C7704` (Sonsbeck command post, 19 February, 0.5) is preferred in conflict group G058; map row `C5809` (Varsselder, 20 February, 0.3) is the alternate, unchanged and not deleted. `C7499` (the Stacey sector row) is not grouped (a different event type) and gets `R806` appended, confidence held at 0.3.

247. **LXIII Corps, late February 1945 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** The 12th Army Group map row `C6058` (`R781`, 0.3) puts the corps at Freiburg im Breisgau on 27 February; Stacey (`R668`, p. 495; new row `C7510`, 0.3 sector row) has the 63rd Corps (Abraham) holding the First Parachute Army's left to south of Roermond on 25 February, about 380 km north. Text row kept; the map row is not changed; speed warning explained in `data/coordinate_exceptions.csv`. Recommendation: put `C6058` and `C7510` in one conflict group with `C7510` preferred, as the corps had moved north to the Roer by February; the Freiburg symbol looks stale from its Alsace period. **Resolved (branch `stacey-victory-campaign`, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** conflict group G056 (C7510 preferred; C6058 alternate, already 0.3); nothing deleted.

248. **4th Canadian Armoured Division, 9-10 April 1945 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** The 12th Army Group map row `C6663` (`R781`/`R782`, 0.5) puts the division at Weijerswold (Coevorden) on 10 April; Stacey (`R668`, p. 558; rows `C7591` Sogel 9 April, `C7598` Sogel counter-attack 10 April, `C7599` Borger 9 April, all 0.5) has its brigades east of the Ems at Sogel and Borger, about 52-57 km north-east. Text rows kept; the map row is not changed. Recommendation: put `C6663` and `C7598` (same date) in one conflict group with the Stacey text row preferred, or lower `C6663` to 0.3; the map symbol is probably the division's rear or the 10th Brigade axis from Wierden. **Resolved (branch `stacey-victory-campaign`, Scott approved 3 November 2026):** conflict group G057 (C7598 preferred; C6663 alternate, confidence unchanged at 0.5; the group is used instead of lowering); nothing deleted.

249. **First Parachute Army (Armeeabteilung Straube), late April - early May 1945 (open, 3 November 2026).** Stacey's Map 14 (new map row `C7681`, 0.3) labels "Armeeabteilung Straube" across the Emden-Wilhelmshaven peninsula (placed at Aurich) for 23 April - 5 May; the existing row `C1238` (`R569`/`R659`, 0.3) has Straube taking over the First Parachute Army on 28 April at a representative point at Bremen, about 110 km east. Both are sector rows at 0.3 and neither is changed. Recommendation: keep both; when a German source for Straube's command is read, move the `C1238` representative point west of the Weser if it confirms the Map 14 sector, and decide whether Armeeabteilung Straube needs its own unit row. Still open (Stacey follow-up, 3 November 2026): data positions unchanged until a German source settles the sector; Armeeabteilung Straube now has its own unit row `GER-AA-STRAUB`; the Map 14 row `C7681` was re-pointed to it and `C1238` (change of commander of the First Parachute Army) stays under `GER-FJA-001A`.

## FMS Fold3 Netherlands pass (B-093, B-717, B-084, B-368; branch `fms-fold3-netherlands`, 3 November 2026): open points

250. **Fall of Xanten, 7-8 March 1945 (open, 3 November 2026).** Meindl (`R806`, English p.17) says Xanten was lost on the evening of 7 March; Stacey (`R668`, pp. 517-519; `C1371`, `C7556`, `C7557`) has the 2nd Canadian and 43rd Divisions take it on 8 March. No row records the German loss of the town, so there is no conflict in the data. Recommendation: keep Stacey's 8 March as the date of capture; note Meindl's date in the B-093 reading notes only; reopen if a German war diary dates it. Updated (FMS B-084 re-read, 3 November 2026): Schlemm (`R777`, English p.9, para 19) places the British-Canadian capture of Xanten in the heavy fighting of 2-3 March, earlier than both Meindl and Stacey; his narrative is written from memory without war diaries. Recommendation unchanged: keep Stacey's 8 March; the two FMS dates (one family) do not outweigh the official history.

251. **II Parachute Corps takes the Wesel bridgehead, 6 or 7 March 1945 (open, 3 November 2026).** Meindl (`R806`, English p.17; new row `C7710`, 0.3) has the corps take over the XLVII Panzer Corps sector and become commander of the Wesel bridgehead on 6 March, command post at Perrich (read from "Ferrich"); `C2234` (`R777`, 0.7) dates the command to 7 March at Birten. Both are FMS (one source family), different dates and places, so no conflict group. Recommendation: keep both; if the First Parachute Army war diary is read, date the handover and lower the row it contradicts.

252. **Army Group H handed to Blaskowitz, 28-31 January 1945 (open, 3 November 2026).** Student (`R807`, English p.20) says he handed over Army Group H to Blaskowitz on 31 January and returned to Berlin the next day; `C1128` (0.3) dates the change to 28 January and `C7443` (Stacey, 0.3) to 29 January, both at Utrecht. No row added (Student gives no place). Recommendation: keep both rows; the order dates the appointment and Student the handover, so add Student's date as a note on `C7443` if Scott wants, or leave them unchanged until the OKW war diary (`R593`) is checked for the date.

253. **XLVII Panzer Corps and 15th Panzergrenadier Division, late October 1944 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** The new Meijel rows `C7741` and `C7742` (`R807`, 27 October, 0.3) and Ellis (`C2056`, 14 October, Venlo) put the corps and division on the Peel front, but the 12th Army Group map rows `C3681`, `C3823` (corps) and `C3679` (division) (`R781`, 24 and 31 October) still show them in Lorraine, about 310-330 km south (`check_coordinates.py` speed warnings). Recommendation: put the map rows in conflict groups as alternates, with the Peel rows preferred (Ellis and Student agree); the map symbols look stale from the units' Lorraine period. Left for Scott's approval, since the map rows are not named in this task. Also (FMS B-084 re-read): the LXIII Corps map rows `C5924` and `C6192` (`R781`, Alsace) now give speed warnings against the new B-084 rows `C7753` and `C7762` (Kaldenkirchen and Wesel, about 380-400 km north), the same stale pattern already grouped for `C6058` in G056. Recommendation: group them as alternates in the same way after Scott approves. **Resolved (Scott approved 3 November 2026, message t293u; branch `fms-fold3-netherlands`):** conflict group G061 with `C7741` (Student, text, Meijel 27 October) preferred and the map rows `C3681` and `C3823` alternate; G062 with `C7742` preferred and `C3679` alternate; the LXIII Corps map rows `C5924` and `C6192` added to G056 as alternates (`C7510` stays preferred, beside `C6058`). Nothing deleted; all confidences unchanged (0.3); old and new values in each row's notes. `C2056` (Ellis, 14 October, Venlo) is not grouped: it is a different date and agrees with the preferred rows.

254. **6th Parachute Division gives up the Kapelsche Veer, mid- or end of January 1945 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** Langhaeuser (`R808`, German p.8; new row `C7790`, 15 January, 0.3) says the bridgehead was given up voluntarily on higher orders in mid-January; Stacey (`C7442`, `R668`, 0.5) has it evacuated on the night of 30-31 January. **Resolved (branch `fms-fold3-netherlands`):** conflict group G059, with `C7442` preferred (an official history from contemporary records) and `C7790` alternate (0.3, unchanged); nothing deleted. `C7438` (the division at the bridgehead from 31 December) raised 0.5 to 0.7 by the corroboration rule (Stacey and B-368).

255. **6th Parachute Division relieves the 84th Division staff, about 10 or 16 February 1945 (closed 3 November 2026; opened 3 November 2026).** Langhaeuser (`R808`, German p.9; new row `C7791`, about 10 February, 0.3) has the division command echelon relieve Fiebig's staff as Kampfgruppe Plocher; Stacey (`C2027`, 0.5) dates the relief to 16 February. **Resolved:** conflict group G060, with `C2027` preferred and `C7791` alternate (0.3, unchanged). Recommendation if reopened: the 16th Parachute Regiment was already fighting at Kleve on 10 February (`C7459`), so Langhaeuser may be dating the regiments' move rather than the staff's.

256. **711th Infantry Division leaves the Waal, 11 or 26 December 1944 (open, 3 November 2026).** Langhaeuser (`R808`; new row `C7786`, 11 December, 0.3) has the 6th Parachute Division take over the 711th Division sector near Heesselt the night after its relief at Arnhem (about 11 December); Stacey (`C7437`, 26 December) dates the 711th's withdrawal later. The rows may record different steps (the handover of the sector, then the division's departure), so they are not grouped. Recommendation: keep both; check the LXXXVIII Corps or Twenty-Fifth Army war diary when read.

257. **256th Volksgrenadier Division arrives, 15 or 23 October 1944 (open, 3 November 2026).** Prittwitz (`R809`, German p.4; new row `C7801`, 15 October, 0.3) has the first infantry of the new division arrive on 15 October; Zangen (`C2176`, `R771`, 0.3) has the first parts relieve the 245th near Tilburg on 23 October. Both are FMS (one family); they may date the first detrainment and the first relief in line. Recommendation: keep both ungrouped; check the Fifteenth Army war diary (NARA T-312) when read.

258. **Reinhard replaced by Schwalbe at LXXXVIII Corps, 22 or 24 December 1944 (open, 3 November 2026).** Prittwitz (`R809`, German p.18; new row `C7825`, 22 December, 0.5) dates the change to 22 December; Stacey (`C7435`, `R668`, 0.3) has 23-24 December. Recommendation: put the rows in a conflict group only if a third source dates it; otherwise keep both, since the corps operations officer is likely to know the day but Stacey may be dating the formal appointment. Also noted, not opened: Prittwitz has 's-Hertogenbosch evacuated on 1 November (`C7812`) while Ellis (`C1470`) has the 53rd Division take it on 27 October; the German row records the 59th Division's final withdrawal over the canal, so both are kept. Updated (FMS B-156 reading, 3 November 2026): Reinhard himself (`R811`, German p.30) says he was transferred to the OKH officer reserve on 21 December and handed over to Schwalbe on 24 December, which supports Stacey's 24 December; B-156 is appended to both `C7825` and `C7435` (no confidence change). Recommendation (updated): a third source now dates the handover, so put `C7825` and `C7435` in a conflict group with `C7435` (24 December) preferred and `C7825` (22 December) alternate, after Scott approves; data unchanged for now.

259. **Capitulation of Festung Holland, 5, 7 or 10 May 1945 (open, 3 November 2026).** Prittwitz (`R810`, German p.8) ends with the capitulation of Festung Holland on 10 May; the dataset has the surrender signed at Wageningen on 5 May (`C1365`, 0.7) and the LXXXVIII Corps area taken over about Utrecht on 7 May (`C7663`, 0.3). No row added for 10 May. Recommendation: keep the existing rows; the 10 May date is probably the formal end (disarmament) as remembered in 1947; add a row only if Scott wants the end date of the corps recorded from B-762.

260. **LXXXVIII Corps command post: Dongen and Herpt, September-October 1944 (open, 3 November 2026).** Reinhard (`R811`, German pp.15, 18) moves the command post from Moergestel to Dongen on 19 or 20 September (new row `C7871`, 0.3) and from Dongen to Herpt at the end of September (new row `C7880`, 30 September, 0.3). B-475 (`C2166`, `C2167`) has Moergestel on 23 September and Dongen about 24 September; NARA T-311 roll 3 (`C7106`) and B-774 (`C7795`) have Dongen on 28 September and 1 October; B-774 (`C7806`) has the move to Herpt on 27 October. All rows kept, not grouped. Recommendation: the Dongen date difference (19-20 against 23-24 September) is small and can stay; put `C7880` in a conflict group as an alternate with `C7795` (Dongen, 1 October) preferred, since a contemporary record and the operations officer both keep the command post at Dongen until late October and Reinhard dates several October events weeks early (also the attack on 's-Hertogenbosch, which he puts in the first third of October against 22-28 October in Ellis and B-774; B-156 appended to `C7805`, `C7807`, `C1469`, no new row).

261. **Division Group Chill leaves for Bergen op Zoom, 7-8 or 20-21 October 1944 (open, 3 November 2026).** Reinhard (`R811`, German p.22; new row `C7884`, 20 October, 0.3) has Chill pulled out of the Goirle area on 20/21 October for a crisis south of Bergen op Zoom; B-774 (`C7800`) has the 85th Division released to LXVII Corps and trucked to Bergen op Zoom on 7 October, and Stacey (`C7349`, `C1796`) has the group at Woensdrecht on 8-10 October. Not grouped (different dates). Recommendation: put `C7884` in a conflict group as an alternate with `C7349` (Stacey, contemporary records) preferred, after Scott approves; Reinhard may be remembering a later move of a remaining part.

262. **6th Parachute Division comes under LXXXVIII Corps, end of November or 19 December 1944 (open, 3 November 2026).** Reinhard (`R811`, German p.28; new rows `C7893` and `C7896`, 30 November, 0.3) has the division on the Lek either side of Arnhem subordinated toward the end of November, giving the corps three divisions; B-774 (`C7823`, 0.5) has the corps take over the Lek sector with the 6th Parachute Division on 19 December. B-368 (`C7779`) has the division take over the Arnhem sector on 19 November under another corps. Not grouped. Recommendation: keep both; the corps commander's date may be the planning order and the operations officer's the formal takeover; check the Army Group H war diary if read.

263. **245th Infantry Division, mid-September 1944: South Beveland or Bladel (open, 3 November 2026).** Reinhard (`R811`, German pp.11-12; new row `C7865`, about 16 September, 0.3) has the 245th arrive at the corps from the Scheldt about 16 September and go into line east of Bladel-Wintelre; the CMH row `C0893` (`R443`, 17 September, 0.3) has it installed on South Beveland. Not grouped. Recommendation: keep both until a contemporary source is read; parts of the division may have stayed on the peninsula while the rest crossed (Stacey and the Army Group B reports may settle it).

264. **II Parachute Corps hands the Reichswald to LXXXVI Corps: end of November or mid-December 1944 (open, 3 November 2026).** Blauensteiner (`R813`, German pp.5-6; new rows `C7917` II Parachute Corps and `C7918` LXXXVI Corps, 30 November, 0.3) has the corps relieved by LXXXVI Corps at the end of November and taking over Roermond-Venlo, with its command post at Vorst at the beginning of December (`C7922`) and moved to St. Hubert in mid-December. Meindl (B-093, `C7701`, 15 December; `C7697`, command post in a Reichswald bunker 25 November) dates the takeover to mid-December. Both are FMS (same family); the 12th Army Group maps (`C4286`-`C4547`) keep the corps at one place from 28 November. Not grouped. Recommendation: keep both until a contemporary record (Army Group H or First Parachute Army war diary) is read; the move to St. Hubert in mid-December, on which both agree, suggests the handover was completed then, and Blauensteiner's end of November may be the order date.
265. **Schlemm out of action, Blumentritt and Kleffel take over: 21, 24/25 or 28 March 1945 (open, 3 November 2026).** Geyer (`R814`, B-414, German p.12 and Anlage 1 notes 7 and 10) has Schlemm put out of action ("ausgefallen" - the English translation's "killed" is wrong) by a bomb hit on 24 March, Straube deputising for a few days, and Blumentritt leading the First Parachute Army and Kleffel the Twenty-fifth Army from 25 March (new rows `C7955`, `C7954`). Stacey (`C7568`) has Schlemm wounded on 21 March; the Lexikon (`C1193`, 20 March) and CMH (`C1192`, 28 March; `C1194` Blumentritt leaving the Twenty-fifth Army 28 March) differ in group G027. B-414 is a third date, same FMS family as the other manuscripts. Not added to G027. Recommendation: check the OKW war diary or the Army Group H war diary for the bomb hit and the appointment dates. Update (B-365, `R819`, 3 November 2026): Blumentritt himself dates the telephone order and his handover to Kleffel 27 March (new row `C8037`, 0.3), a fourth date; same FMS family as B-414; still not grouped.
266. **245th Infantry Division in early March 1945: Alsace or Coesfeld (open, 3 November 2026).** B-414 (`C7950`) says the division has been in army group reserve about Coesfeld-Billerbeck-Dülmen "since 5 March"; the 12th Army Group map row `C6166` (6 March) still shows it near Mertzwiller in Alsace. Speed exception added; both rows kept. Recommendation: check the division's transfer north (Tessin; OB West situation maps of early March). Update (B-147, `R815`): Geyer's Rhineland report has the division arriving about Borken to refit in the first days of March (`C8001`); a second speed exception (`C8001`/`C6032`, map row of 27 February) added. Both FMS rows are one family; still open.
267. **6th Parachute Division, March 1945: Elden or Millingen, Emmerich or Terborg (open, 3 November 2026).** B-453 (`C8005`, `C8010`, `C8011`) puts the division on the Rhine at Millingen from about 11 March and behind the Oude IJssel at Terborg-Varsseveld from 26 March; the map rows `C6213`/`C6348` (R781) keep it at Elden (Arnhem) on 13 and 20 March, and Stacey (`C7574`) has it with the 346th at Emmerich on 28 March. Possibly parts left behind or map lag; check the Canadian war diaries or the R781 overlay before grouping.
268. **6th Parachute Division, April 1945: dating of the Holten line and the IJssel west bank (open, 3 November 2026).** B-580 (`C8014`, `C8017`) is faint: the Holten line reads "10." in the German and 14 April in the English, the move behind the IJssel "13.4."/"15.4." in the German and "18 [?] April" in the English; B-762 (`C7839`, `C7840`) has LXXXVIII Corps on the Holten line on 10 April and leaving Zwolle on 14 April. Rows kept at the German readings; check the Canadian war diaries (4th and 5th Armoured, 1st Canadian Division) before regrouping.
269. **711th Infantry Division in the Netherlands in February 1945? (open, 3 November 2026).** B-365 (`R819`, German p.12, Skizze 1) names divisions 711 and 712 ("in name only") on the Twenty-fifth Army water front on 3 February 1945 (new row `C8032`, toward Rotterdam on the hand sketch); Stacey (`C7437`) has the 711th ordered to Hungary on 26 December 1944 and the last map rows are of 9 January. Possibly a memory slip or a residual staff. Row kept at 0.3; check Tessin or the OB West situation maps of February 1945.
270. **I Canadian Corps opposite the Twenty-fifth Army before mid-March 1945 (open, 3 November 2026).** B-365 (`C8038`) says only the I Canadian Corps with four divisions and the 1st Polish Division was identified opposite the army south front during 3 February-27 March; the corps came under First Canadian Army only on 15 March (`C1361`, `C7578`), so for February the author probably means II Canadian Corps. Row dated 20 March as an inference; also the 346th Division at Schouwen-Goeree early February (`C8030`) against the map rows in the Hoeksche Waard (`C5467`, `C5600`). Recommendation: check the First Canadian Army order of battle for February 1945.
271. **272nd Volksgrenadier Division, mid-December 1944: Bergstein or Simmerath (open, 4 November 2026).** B-811 German (`R820`, p.24; new row `C8061`) has the division brought from the Simmerath area to attack over the Kall toward Bergstein about three times after about 10 December; MacDonald (`C0987`) has it holding Wahlerscheid-Simmerath on 16 December. Possibly part of the division only, or the attacks ended before 16 December. Not grouped (different dates). Recommendation: check the LXXIV Corps or 272nd Division files (NARA T-314/T-315) or MacDonald, The Siegfried Line Campaign, ch. 24.
272. **LXXIV Corps to the Fifth Panzer Army: 4 or 5 February 1945 (open, 4 November 2026).** B-811 German (p.59, in `C8077`) says the Fifth Panzer Army took over the corps early on 4 February; `C1146` (CMH) gives 5 February. No new row for the handover; not grouped. Recommendation: check the Army Group B war diary.
273. **One manuscript, two references: B-811 English (`R768`) and German original (`R820`) (open, 4 November 2026, for Scott).** R768 was read in the English translation on 30 October 2026; R820 is the German original read on 4 November 2026 (different Fold3 file). Both are the same manuscript, same family; R820 has been appended to the R768 rows without raising anything. Recommendation: keep both (they cite different scans and page systems), or merge R820 into R768 if Scott prefers one reference per manuscript.

274. **Panzer Lehr Division, 24-28 February 1945: Rheindahlen, north of Mönchengladbach, Garzweiler or Moers (open, 3 November 2026).** B-812 German (`R821`, pp.-14- and -18-; new rows `C8099`, `C8100`) has the first elements arriving north of Mönchengladbach on the afternoon of 27 February and attacking from north-east of Rheindahlen toward Garzweiler on 28 February (Anlage 7d: under the army from 27 February). `C7990` (R815) has OB West taking it to Rheindahlen on 24 February; `C7757` (R777) has it as the only division of Abraham's corps (Moers sector) on 28 February. All FMS; not grouped (the 28 February places may be the attack and a later assignment). Recommendation: check the Army Group B or OB West situation maps of 27 February-1 March 1945.
275. **US 3rd and 8th Armored Divisions on 25 February 1945 (open, 3 November 2026).** B-812 German (p.-9-, `C8113`) has the US 3rd Armored thrusting from Niederzier toward Steinstrass on 25 February; `C1838` (R062) places it at Lierneux at the end of a month of rest on 25 February. B-812 (English p.9) also names the 8th Armored before Erkelenz on 25 February (not entered: German identification only in the English check), against `C0610` (a combat command alerted to cross the Roer on 27 February). Rows kept at 0.3; not grouped. Recommendation: check the 3rd Armored Division after-action report for 23-26 February.
276. **Fifteenth Army change of command and new command post: 28 February or 1 March 1945 (open, 3 November 2026).** B-812 German p.-19- (`C8108`, north of Münstereifel, 28 February) dates the order and the move to 28 February; `C1158` (R540, R539) dates the exchange with the Fifth Panzer Army 1 March. The German page -19- was not re-checked on the viewer (the English p.26 was), so 0.3. Also not entered: Volks Artillery Corps 766 (not legible) and the index author and date (not re-checked). Recommendation: re-view Fold3 page 60-61 and the index pages 39-40.
277. **Places removed by open-cast mining (open, 3 November 2026).** Garzweiler (`PL3648`) and Harff (`PL3649`) points are the present localities; the 1945 villages were removed by lignite mining (like Pier, `PL3628`). Recommendation: if Scott wants historic points, take them from the 1:25,000 sheets of the period.
278. **XLVII Panzer Corps staff on 11 March 1945: Silvolde or south-east of Bocholt (open, 4 November 2026).** B-197 German (`R822`, p.-1-; new row `C8120`) moves the corps to Silvolde on 11 March; `C7718` (R806) has the staff at a farm south-east of Bocholt on 11 March before moving south. Both FMS; possibly a stop on the way. Not grouped. Recommendation: check the Army Group H war diary or situation maps of 10-12 March.
279. **B-197 and the "MS # B-198" header (open, 4 November 2026, for Scott).** The B-197 text pages carry the typed header "MS # B-198" and Skizze 1 "MS # B-197 (B-198)"; B-200 cites the same report as B-198. No separate B-198 or B-199 images were found in the Fold3 run (pages 75-114). Recorded as one reference `R822` under B-197. Recommendation: confirm against the FMS catalogue whether B-198 is the same report (as assumed) or a lost separate study; if B-198 turns up separately, record it as a second reference of the same family without raises.
280. **Uncertain readings in B-197 left out (open, 4 November 2026).** Corps command post 26 March "Hülsdau" (5 km from Dorsten), the 116th Panzer Division command post "Westen" on 25 March, the commander labels on Skizze 1, and "Bockum, 3 km west of Datteln" (`C8128`, entered at Datteln at 0.3; no Bockum found there). Also LIII/LXIII corps numbers on pp.-9-, -17- and -18-. Recommendation: re-view Fold3 pages 85-87, 95-96 and 102 at higher zoom.
281. **B-200 poorly legible line names (open, 4 November 2026).** B-200 answer 5 (`R823`, Fold3 page 116) names the 30 March line with poorly legible places ("Dreweri? - Hasel? - Rastrup? - Haecklinghausen? - Zeche Lenin?"); the row `C8150` takes the B-197 reading (Drewer-Hüls-west edge of Recklinghausen-Zeche Ewald-Zeche Graf Bismarck) at 0.3. "Hottrop" read as Bottrop and "Gueden" (start of the attack toward Münster) not identified. Recommendation: re-view Fold3 pages 115-116 at higher zoom.
282. **9th Panzer Division counterattack on Lindern, 30 November 1944: retaken or failed (open, 4 November 2026).** B-811 German (`C8048`, R820) has the division retaking Lindern; B-367 (`C8169`, R824, pp.23-24) has the attack at about 1900 entering Lindern but not holding it and being called off on 1 December. Same date and place, so no conflict group; listed in `claim_key_exceptions.csv`. MacDonald has Lindern in American hands from 29 November. Recommendation: Scott to decide whether to lower `C8048` or mark it superseded.
283. **B-367 internal inconsistencies (open, 4 November 2026).** Heavy tank battalion 507 (p.7) against 506 (pp.12, 15, 17; also Heeres-Panzer-Abteilung 509 on p.18); Panzer Regiment 3 (p.12) against 9 (p.14) for the 9th Panzer Division; "18.12" (p.7) for 18 November; "29./30.12" (p.11, Brachelen bridge) for 29-30 November; title on the index sheet "1945" for 1944. No rows depend on them. Also not entered: corps command post "Muckelhoven"/Hückelhoven (17 November) and "Cuckum" (2 December), both uncertain readings. Recommendation: re-view Fold3 pages 11, 12, 17, 18 and 35.
284. **B-368 re-read (German original primary) and its overlaps (open, 4 November 2026).** B-368 was already reference `R808` (read 3 November 2026 from the same Fold3 file, German text and English translation), so no new reference id. The re-read added one row (`C8177`, 346th Division arriving about Emmerich about 10 February) and two corroborations (`C1369`, `C1380`). The 346th row sits between `C8030` (Schouwen-Goeree, 3 February, R819) and `C7465` (a regiment east of Bedburg, 12 February, R668/R815). Overlap with B-453 (`R817`) and B-580 (`R818`, same author) is only at 10-11 March: B-368 and B-453 agree on the Haffen-Marienbaum/Hamminkeln-Birten sector (`C7794`, `C8005`). Internal points, no rows affected: IV./Fallschirm-Artillerie-Regiment "6" (German p.6) against "16" (p.11, also in the English); Geyer note dated 18.2.1947 at the top and 17.2.1947 at the bottom; English "wiped out" for German "verbraucht" (Regiment 16, February); the narrative ends on 10 March although the title runs to 10 May. Not entered: Brigade Irene, 4th Canadian Armoured and 1st Polish Armoured opposite the division December-January (no dated place). Recommendation: none needed unless Scott wants Brigade Irene placed from Stacey.
285. **B-369 uncertain place names (open, 4 November 2026).** Degener (`R825`) names many villages west of Héricourt that are hard to read in the scan ("Chuinot", "Bzans", "Vernans", "Mushie/Buhle", "Bois du Chenon/Chanois", "Fort Bermont", "Brebotte", "Tretudans", "Lure/Isire"); only places confirmed on the map were entered (11 rows `C8178`-`C8188`). The division command post before 14 November ("Champey") is mentioned but not entered. No French units are named, so no Allied rows. Recommendation: re-view Fold3 pages 7, 13, 16-18 and 23-24 at higher zoom, or check against de Lattre's history for the French units opposite (French 1st Armoured and 2nd Moroccan Divisions likely).
286. **B-674 faint German scan; command-post names from the English (open, 4 November 2026).** Meindl's Part IV (`R826`) is very faint in the German; the corps command posts "Ochhin" near Gronau (31 March-3 April), Emsbüren (3-5 April, German first read "Emsdetten"), "Niederdorf" (5-8 April), "Uploh" (8-11 April), "Hollener-Moos" (11-20 April) and Jübberde (21-30 April) and the 5 April date heading are taken from the English translation. Not entered: Ostendorf (27/28-30 March, first digit unclear), Niederdorf and Uploh (not identified), the "Jasenburg" breakthrough of 28/29 March (perhaps Isselburg), "Edderrecht" and the "Siegen" of the English (perhaps Lingen). `C8193` (Hollenermoor, Saterland) is an inferred reading (Hollen in Uplengen is the alternative). Scott: confirm or identify these names on a period map.
287. **B-674 against R814 for the corps in mid and late April 1945 (open, 4 November 2026).** B-674 has the II Parachute Corps command post at Hollener-Moos to 20 April and Jübberde 21-30 April, holding Leer-Papenburg-Friesoythe-Vehne-Moos until ordered behind the Elbe at the end of April; `C7979` (R814) has the corps beginning its move north toward Cuxhaven-Bremerhaven on 18 April, and `C7643` (R668) still credits it with the 8th Parachute Division on 27 April, whereas B-674 hands that division to LXXXVI Corps about 10 April. Kept side by side; Scott to decide whether a conflict group is wanted.
288. **B-674 source-internal discrepancies (open, 4 November 2026).** Meindl says Schlemm fell ill and Student took over; Geyer's supplement says Schlemm was wounded in an air attack and replaced by Straube (24 March) and then Blumentritt (26/28 March), with "Gruppe Student" formed 1 April. The cover letter puts Straube at LXXXVIII Corps, Geyer at LXXXVI Corps. The 3,500-4,000 men per division are head-count or ration strength in Meindl but combat strength in Geyer (notes only). The 21st Parachute Division (no unit file) holds Leer-Papenburg-Küsten Canal from about 10 April; not entered pending a unit row.
289. **B-327 strengths and capitulation place against B-674 (open, 4 November 2026).** Blauensteiner (`R827`, p.1) gives about 1,000 men per parachute division on 10 March; Meindl (B-674, `R826`) gives 3,500-4,000 (head-count or ration strength), which Geyer calls combat strength; strengths stay in notes. B-327 puts the corps capitulation at "Grossbuedelsdorf" (reading uncertain; Büdelsdorf?) 15 km east of Schleswig; B-674 has Gross Breckendorf south-east of Schleswig (`C8197`, 0.3); no B-327 row entered. Scott: decide which place to prefer.
290. **B-327 facts not entered (open, 4 November 2026).** The 6th Parachute Division subordinated to LXXXVI Corps after the Isselburg breakthrough (end of March, no place); brigade "Grossdeutschland" north-east of Rheine early April (no unit file); Lingen lost about 6 April (Allied unit not named); 21st Parachute Division formed from a weak regiment from Holland via Papenburg and pushed back to the Leda about 20 April (no unit file); 7th Parachute Division held at the Elbe near Harburg; the corps ordered to Reinbek before the Lauenburg attack. Geyer notes the 21st was only a battle group.
